{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We prove nonuniqueness for the periodic hard-sphere Boltzmann equation by constructing two distinct global renormalized solutions with the same nonnegative initial density on $`\\mathbb T^3\\times\\mathbb R^3`$. This density has bounded velocity support and finite mass, energy, and absolute entropy. Both solutions conserve local mass and total momentum and satisfy the global energy and entropy-dissipation inequalities. On a common initial interval, they are strongly continuous in <i>L</i><sup>1</sup>, and their collision gains and losses are integrable.",

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove nonuniqueness for the periodic hard-sphere Boltzmann equation by constructing two distinct global renormalized solutions with the same nonnegative initial density on $`\mathbb T^3\times\mathbb R^3`$. This density has bounded velocity support and finite mass, energy, and absolute entropy. Both solutions conserve local mass and total momentum and satisfy the global energy and entropy-dissipation inequalities. On a common initial interval, they are strongly continuous in <i>L</i><sup>1</sup>, and their collision gains and losses are integrable.

- **claim-same-datum-nonuniqueness** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There is a nonnegative density $F_0$ on $\mathbb T^3\times\mathbb R^3$, with bounded velocity support and $$\int_{\mathbb T^3\times\mathbb R^3} F_0(1+\|v\|^2+\|\log F_0\|)\,\mathrm dx\,\mathrm dv<\infty,$$ for which the hard-sphere Boltzmann equation has two distinct global admissible solutions, in the sense of Definition <a href="#def:admissible" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:admissible">2</a>, with initial datum $F_0$. On some common initial interval both solutions are strongly continuous in $L^1$, and their collision gains and losses are integrable in space-time-velocity.
  - Explanation: A spatially concentrated datum admits distinct global admissible evolutions. Strong continuity and integrable collision terms hold on a common initial interval.
- **claim-carleman-collision-geometry** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-carleman)): For the normalization in <a href="#eq:collision" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:collision">[eq:collision]</a>, the symmetric bilinear gain is $$\label{ker:carleman} P(c,h)(v) =2\int_{\mathbb R^3}c(p)\,\,\mathrm dp \int_{\mathbb R^3} \delta\bigl((v-p)\cdot(\xi-v)\bigr)h(\xi)\,\,\mathrm d\xi.$$ For fixed $p\ne v$, the inner delta denotes surface measure on the plane $(v-p)\cdot(\xi-v)=0$, divided by $\|v-p\|$. For incoming velocities $p,\xi$, the outgoing measure has total mass $2\pi\|p-\xi\|$ and is uniform on the sphere with diameter $[p,\xi]$. Formula <a href="#ker:carleman" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ker:carleman">[ker:carleman]</a> also defines the gain when $c$ is a nonnegative velocity measure and $h$ is a nonnegative density, whenever the displayed integral is finite almost everywhere.
  - Explanation: The gain has an exact plane representation with the specified collision normalization, and extends to a cold velocity measure when the integral is finite almost everywhere.
- **claim-geometric-slice-uniformity** (bound, [source](#stmt-ker-geometric-slice)): Let $\lambda$ be a probability mixture of the measures $\mathcal U_d$, $d>0$, and $\delta_0$, and let $\rho$ be any axial probability measure. No bound on its density or its support is required. Put $c=\lambda\otimes\rho$. For $R\geq1$, $\bar v\ne0$, and an interval $I\subset\mathbb R$ of length $0<\ell\leq2R$, one has $$\begin{aligned} &\frac{1}{W(v)}\int c(\,\mathrm dp) \int_{\|\xi-v\|\leq R} \delta\bigl((v-p)\cdot(\xi-v)\bigr)W(\xi)\,\,\mathrm d\xi \leq C_q R^2,\label{ker:geometric-total}\\ &\frac{1}{W(v)}\int c(\,\mathrm dp) \int_{\substack{\|\xi-v\|\leq R\\ \xi_3\in I}} \delta\bigl((v-p)\cdot(\xi-v)\bigr)W(\xi)\,\,\mathrm d\xi \leq C_q\bigl(R\ell+R^{q-1}\ell^{2-q}\bigr). \label{ker:geometric-interval} \end{aligned}$$ The constants are independent of all transverse widths and of $\rho$. In particular, these statements hold with $\lambda=\mathcal G_d$ for every $d\geq0$.
  - Explanation: Transverse averaging controls total plane mass and axial slices without imposing axial density or support bounds.
- **claim-uniform-sheet-gain** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-sheet-bound)): For every $L<\infty$ there is $C_{q,m,L}<\infty$ such that $$\label{ker:sheet-gain} P(c,H)(v)\leq C_{q,m,L}H(v) \quad\hbox{for almost every }v, \qquad c\in\mathcal S_L.$$ More quantitatively, for $R\geq1$, $$\label{ker:shell-tail} \mathcal K_c\bigl(v; R<\|p\|+\|\xi-v\|\leq2R\bigr) \leq C_{q,m,L}R^2e^{-cR^2},$$ where $c>0$ is an absolute constant. The same conclusions, multiplied by $A_0$, hold under sheet domination with amplitude $A_0$.
  - Explanation: Sheet domination gives a uniform weighted gain bound and exponentially decreasing normalized shell masses, with linear dependence on the domination amplitude.
- **claim-normalized-kernel-tightness** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-kernel-tightness)): Fix $L<\infty$. The following conclusions hold uniformly for $c\in\mathcal S_L$ and for outgoing velocities off the axis.* 1. *The tails in $\|p\|+\|\xi-v\|$ are uniformly negligible: $$\label{ker:uniform-tail} \lim_{R\to\infty}\sup_{c\in\mathcal S_L}\mathop{\mathrm{ess\,sup}}_v \mathcal K_c(v;\|p\|+\|\xi-v\|>R)=0.$$ Also, $$\label{ker:large-output} \lim_{M\to\infty}\sup_{c\in\mathcal S_L} \mathop{\mathrm{ess\,sup}}_{\|v\|>M}\mathcal K_c(v;\mathbb R^3\times\mathbb R^3)=0.$$* 2. *For every fixed $M,\delta>0$, $$\begin{aligned} &\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow0} \sup_{c\in\mathcal S_L} \mathop{\mathrm{ess\,sup}}_{\substack{\|v\|\leq M\\0<\|\bar v\|\leq\varepsilon}} \mathcal K_c(v;\|\bar\xi\|\geq\delta)=0, \label{ker:axis-input}\\ &\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow0} \sup_{c\in\mathcal S_L} \mathop{\mathrm{ess\,sup}}_{\substack{\|v\|\leq M\\0<\|\bar v\|\leq\varepsilon}} \mathcal K_c(v;\|v_3-p_3\|\geq\delta)=0. \label{ker:axis-mismatch} \end{aligned}$$* 3. *The axial input marginals are uniformly non-atomic. More precisely, for each $R\geq1$ and each interval $I$ of length $0<\ell\leq1$, $$\label{ker:axial-interval} \mathcal K_c(v;\|p\|+\|\xi-v\|\leq R,\ \xi_3\in I) \leq C_{q,m,L,R}\bigl(\ell+\ell^{2-q}\bigr).$$ In combination with <a href="#ker:uniform-tail" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ker:uniform-tail">[ker:uniform-tail]</a>, this gives $$\label{ker:axial-modulus} \lim_{\ell\downarrow0}\sup_{c\in\mathcal S_L}\mathop{\mathrm{ess\,sup}}_v \sup_{\|I\|\leq\ell}\mathcal K_c(v;\xi_3\in I)=0.$$* 4. *Let $\mathcal S_L(\varepsilon)$ denote the subclass whose elementary transverse widths are at most $\varepsilon$. For every $M<\infty$ and $r_0>0$, $$\label{ker:off-axis-small-input} \lim_{\delta\downarrow0}\limsup_{\varepsilon\downarrow0} \sup_{c\in\mathcal S_L(\varepsilon)} \mathop{\mathrm{ess\,sup}}_{\substack{\|v\|\leq M\\\|\bar v\|\geq r_0}} \mathcal K_c(v;\|\bar\xi\|<\delta)=0.$$* *All these conclusions hold under uniformly bounded sheet amplitudes.
  - Explanation: The complete normalized kernels have uniform tails, non-atomic axial marginals, and complementary estimates near and away from the velocity axis. Small transverse inputs away from the axis require shrinking sheet widths.
- **claim-gaussian-parameter-sheet-domination** (bound, [source](#stmt-ker-parameter-sheets)): Let a measure in parameters $w=(\bar w,w_3)\in\mathbb R^2\times\mathbb R$ have density $j$ satisfying $$0\leq j(w)\leq C_0\pi^{-3/2}e^{-\|w\|^2}.$$ Let its velocity image be given by $$\label{ker:parameter-map} \bar p=d\bar w,\qquad p_3=\Phi(w_3),\qquad 0\leq d\leq\tfrac1{16},\qquad \|\Phi(a)\|\leq A+\delta\|a\|,\quad0\leq\delta\leq\tfrac1{16},$$ where $\Phi$ is merely measurable. The image measure is dominated by $C_0$ times a sheet in $\mathcal S_{L_A}$, with $L_A$ depending only on $A$. Its gain satisfies Lemma <a href="#lem:sheet-bound" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:sheet-bound">6</a> with a constant depending only on $q,m,A,C_0$.* *If $c_{>B}$ is the image of the restricted parameter measure $\mathbf 1_{\|w\|>B}j(w)\,\,\mathrm dw$, then $$\label{ker:parameter-tail} \mathop{\mathrm{ess\,sup}}_v\frac{P(c_{>B},H)(v)}{H(v)} \leq C_{q,m,A,C_0}e^{-B^2/2},\qquad B\geq0.$$ Thus parameter truncations are valid in the normalized collision measure, even when $\Phi$ compresses intervals or has atoms in its image law. Probability mixtures of measurable families with common $q,m,A,C_0$ and the width bounds in <a href="#ker:parameter-map" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ker:parameter-map">[ker:parameter-map]</a> preserve these assertions. More generally, a positive mixing measure of total mass $B_0<\infty$ multiplies the domination amplitude and quantitative upper bounds by $B_0$; the sheet parameter $L_A$ is unchanged. For <a href="#ker:parameter-tail" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ker:parameter-tail">[ker:parameter-tail]</a>, impose the restriction $\|w\|>B$ in each component before mixing. Additional measurable masks taking values in $[0,1]$ preserve the assertions.
  - Explanation: Gaussian parameter majorants remain controlled after measurable axial compression. Parameter-tail removal is valid for the complete normalized gain, including mixtures and measurable masks.
- **claim-hot-hot-gain-majorant** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-hot-bound)): There is a constant $C_{q,m}$ such that $$\label{ker:hot-gain} P(H,H)\leq C_{q,m}H \quad\hbox{almost everywhere}.$$ In fact the proof below works for every $m>5$.
  - Explanation: The singular integrable velocity weight controls its own quadratic gain. The proof also permits the stated broader polynomial decay regime.
- **claim-disjoint-cold-enlargements** (theorem, [source](#stmt-jet-geometry)): For $0\le t\le\overline T$, the sets $\mathcal O_i(t)$ are pairwise disjoint. Moreover, $$\widehat E_j^\sigma\subset \{s_j<\|y\|<e^\eta s_j\}\subset B(0,s_0).$$ At each point of the periodic lift there is therefore at most one label whose enlargement contains that point.
  - Explanation: The spatial enlargements remain disjoint, so a position belongs to at most one enlarged cold label.
- **claim-datum-entropy-and-seed-convergence** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-datum)): The datum <a href="#eq:datum" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:datum">[eq:datum]</a> satisfies $$\int_\Omega F_0(1+\|v\|^2+\|\log F_0\|)\,\,\mathrm dx\,\,\mathrm dv<\infty .$$ Its truncations $F_0^{(N)}$, retaining $j\le N$, converge to $F_0$ in mass, energy, and relative entropy. In addition, if $\chi\ge0$ is a fixed bounded smooth compactly supported function of $(x,v)$, periodized after replacing $x$ by $x/s_N$, and $0\le\lambda_N\le L_0<\infty$, then $$F_0^{(N)}+\lambda_Ns_N\chi(x/s_N,v)$$ has the same limiting mass, energy, and relative entropy as $F_0$. For the last assertion $s_0$ may be decreased so that distinct periodic copies of the spatial support of the seed are disjoint.
  - Explanation: The multiscale datum has the required integrability. Truncations and the allowed localized seeds converge to the same mass, energy, and relative entropy.
- **claim-transported-maxwellian-barrier** (bound, [source](#stmt-jet-barrier)): The constant $C_m$ can be chosen so that $f_i\le m_i$ on $[0,\overline T]$, uniformly in the label. Moreover, almost everywhere along every free characteristic, $$D_tm_i\ge \left(1+\tfrac78 \|w_i\|^2/d_{s_j}^2\right)m_i . \label{jet:barrier-derivative}$$ There is a constant $C_\nu$, independent of the label, such that $$P(m_i,m_i)=m_i\nu_{m_i},\qquad \nu_{m_i}(v)\le C_\nu\frac K{s_j}(d_{s_j}+\|w_i\|). \label{jet:barrier-collision}$$ Every free core remains in its own spatial enlargement: $$f_i(t,x,v)>0\quad\Longrightarrow\quad x\in\mathcal O_i(t).$$
  - Explanation: A Maxwellian barrier dominates each free core, has a favorable transport derivative, and obeys an exact gain-loss identity. Free cores stay inside their own enlargements.
- **claim-uniform-cold-control** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-cold-control)): There is a constant $L$, depending on the fixed parameters, such that after imposing the additional condition $$Ld_0\overline s^9\le1, \label{jet:thermal-comparison}$$ the following statements hold at every time of existence of the finite approximation in $0\le t\le\overline T$.* 1. *For every retained label $i=(j,\sigma,n)$, $$0\le c_i\le f_i+\varepsilon_j m_i,\qquad \varepsilon_j=Ld_{s_j}/s_j . \label{jet:cold-remainder}$$* 2. *At each spatial point call the unique label with $x\in\mathcal O_i(t)$, if one exists and is retained, the main label. Let $c_{\mathrm{main}}$ be that color, or zero, and set $e=c-c_{\mathrm{main}}$. For every $A\ge0$, $$0\le e(t,x,v)\le C_A t^A e^{-4\|v\|^2}. \label{jet:escaped}$$ The upper bound remains valid after restricting the escaped sum to any subset of its labels.* 3. *If the main label has scale $s$, its velocity measure is bounded by a fixed multiple of $(t+s)^{-1}$ times a sum of admissible probability sheets from Lemma <a href="#lem:sheet-bound" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:sheet-bound">6</a>. Its free-core contribution has the exact parameterization <a href="#eq:core-jacobian" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:core-jacobian">[eq:core-jacobian]</a> below. The remainder has the smaller velocity-mass coefficient $Cd_s/s^2$. In particular, for every fixed $k\ge0$, $$\int_{\mathbb R^3}\langle v\rangle^k c_{\mathrm{main}}\,\,\mathrm dv \le\frac{C_k}{t+s},\qquad \int_{\|v\|>R}\langle v\rangle^k c_{\mathrm{main}}\,\,\mathrm dv \le\frac{C_k e^{-c_kR^2}}{t+s}\quad(R\ge3). \label{jet:moment-envelope}$$* 4. *For the mixed-cold source in the hot equation, $$\sum_{\substack{i,j\in\mathcal I_N\\i\ne j}} P^b(c_i,c_j)(t,x,v) \le C_A t^{A-1}H(v)\qquad (t>0) \label{jet:mixed-source}$$ for every $A\ge0$, with constants uniform in $N$ and $b$.
  - Explanation: Each cold color stays close to its free core. Escaped colors are negligible, the main color has controlled sheet and moment envelopes, and collisions between distinct colors give an arbitrarily small initial source.
- **claim-cap-cold-core-survival** (bound, [source](#stmt-jet-survival)): Suppose a finite approximation satisfies $$0\le h(t,x,v)\le(t+s_N)H(v)$$ on an initial interval $[0,T]\subset[0,\overline T]$. All conclusions below concern times $t\in[0,T]$. For every $M\ge0$ there are constants $C,C_M$, independent of $T,N$, the cutoff, and the seed, such that $$\begin{gathered} e^{-E_{s,N}(t)}f_i(t,x,v) \le c_i(t,x,v) \le f_i(t,x,v)+L(d_s/s)m_i(t,x,v),\\ E_{s,N}(t)=C(d_st/s+t^2+s_Nt)+C_Mt^{M+1} \end{gathered} \label{jet:survival-sandwich}$$ for every label of scale $s$. Consequently, if $r\to0$, $s_N\le r$, $s/r\in[\epsilon,R]$ for fixed $0<\epsilon<R<\infty$, and $0\le t\le\min(T,Ar)$, then $$E_{s,N}(t)\le C_{\epsilon,R,A}(d_s+r^2)+C_{M,A}r^{M+1}\longrightarrow0$$ uniformly in the label and in points of its free core. The transverse free displacement divided by the stripe period satisfies $$\frac{td_s}{s^2}\le C_{\epsilon,R,A}r^9\longrightarrow0. \label{jet:stripe-displacement}$$ On these scales the difference between cold and free-core velocity measures also satisfies, uniformly in position and time, $$r\int_{\mathbb R^3}\|c_i-f_i\|(t,x,v)\,\,\mathrm dv\longrightarrow0. \label{jet:survival-measure}$$
  - Explanation: Under the hot cap, cold cores survive with vanishing optical depth on comparable dilated scales. Their thermal displacement becomes negligible relative to stripe periods.
- **claim-dormant-and-cap-approximations** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-finite-cap)): There is a fixed $T_0>0$ such that the following statements hold.* 1. *With $\lambda=0$, the finite approximations exist on $[0,T_0]$ and satisfy $0\le h\le t^2H$.* 2. *There is a fixed finite $L_0$ such that, for every $0<T\le T_0$ and every $N$, some $\lambda\in[0,L_0]$ gives an approximation on $[0,T]$ with $$\label{eq:cap} 0\le h(t,x,v)\le(t+s_N)H(v),\qquad \max_{0\le t\le T}\frac{\\|h(t)\\|_H}{t+s_N}=1.$$* *In both cases all cold components obey Proposition <a href="#prop:cold-control" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:cold-control">14</a>.
  - Explanation: The zero-seed family has a dormant upper bound. A bounded seed parameter can also be selected to attain the linear hot cap on each allowed observation interval.
- **claim-fixed-horizon-admissible-limits** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-admissible-limits)): Fix $T\in(0,T_0]$. Either the zero-seed approximations of Proposition <a href="#prop:finite-cap" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:finite-cap">16</a>, or any sequence of its cap shots on $[0,T]$, has a subsequence whose total densities $F_N=c_N+h_N$ converge weakly in $L^1([0,T]\times\Omega)$ to a nonnegative density $F$. The subsequence can be chosen so that $F_N(t)\rightharpoonup F(t)$ in $L^1(\Omega)$ at every $t\in[0,T]$, with uniformly small polynomially weighted velocity tails. The limit has the common initial datum <a href="#eq:datum" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:datum">[eq:datum]</a>, and $$Q^\pm(F,F)\in L^1([0,T]\times\Omega),\qquad F\in C([0,T];L^1(\Omega)).$$ It satisfies every condition of Definition <a href="#def:admissible" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:admissible">2</a> on this interval, including the inequalities at every time. Each such limit has an admissible global continuation. The dormant upper bound, consisting of the cold majorants of Proposition <a href="#prop:cold-control" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:cold-control">14</a> and $t^2H$, passes to a zero-seed limit.
  - Explanation: At a fixed physical horizon, both approximation families admit admissible limits with the same datum, strong initial continuity, integrable collision terms, and admissible global continuations.
- **claim-uniformly-integrable-velocity-averaging** (theorem, [source](#stmt-comp-averaging)): Suppose that $f_n$ and $g_n$ are locally uniformly integrable in $L^1$ on an open time-space-velocity region, and $(\partial_t+v\cdot\nabla_x)f_n=g_n$ there in distributions. Then for every smooth compactly supported velocity test $\psi$, the averages $\int\psi(v)f_n(t,x,v)\,\mathrm dv$ are relatively compact in local $L^1_{t,x}$ on regions where the hypotheses hold for velocities in a neighborhood of $\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\psi$. The assertion applies both on a spatial torus and locally in $\mathbb R^3$.
  - Explanation: Uniform integrability of a transport solution and its source yields strong local compactness of compactly supported velocity averages.
- **claim-dilated-linear-hot-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-blowup-limit)): Every sequence <a href="#eq:rescaling" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:rescaling">[eq:rescaling]</a> has a subsequence for which $h_{r_N}\rightharpoonup g$ in local spacetime $L^1$ on $(0,U)\times\mathbb R^3_x\times\mathbb R^3_v$, where $$\label{eq:limit-equation} 0\leq g(t,x,v)\leq(t+l)H(v),\qquad D_tg=2P(B,g)-g\nu_B.$$ Here $B$ is <a href="#eq:background" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:background">[eq:background]</a>; its gain and loss are defined by integration against the velocity measure. Both terms on the right are locally integrable, with a velocity majorant uniform in position on each compact positive-time interval. Compactly supported velocity averages of $h_{r_N}$ converge strongly in local $L^1_{t,x}$. The limit has a locally strongly continuous $L^1$ representative at positive times and obeys the integrating-factor formula <a href="#dil:mild" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="dil:mild">[dil:mild]</a> below.* *If $l>0$, pass also to a limit $\lambda_N\to\lambda_0$ of the shot parameters. The conclusion and its local continuity extend to time zero, with $$\label{dil:initial-trace} g(0,x,v)=\lambda_0 l\,\chi(x/l,v).$$
  - Explanation: Cap dilation yields a hot density solving a linear equation against a time-dependent axial background measure. Hot velocity averages converge strongly; a positive lower mesh endpoint also gives an initial trace.
- **claim-every-cap-limit-nonzero** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-nonzero-limit)): Consider any sequence of cap shots with horizons $T_n\to0$, approximation indices $N_n\to\infty$, and $s_{N_n}/T_n\to0$. Make the rescaling <a href="#eq:rescaling" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:rescaling">[eq:rescaling]</a>, and pass to any subsequence supplied by Proposition <a href="#prop:blowup-limit" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:blowup-limit">20</a>. Its limit satisfies $$g\not\equiv0\qquad\text{on }(0,U)\times\mathbb R^3_x\times\mathbb R^3_v.$$ This conclusion includes $l=1$, $U=1$, and $U=\infty$.
  - Explanation: The cap event cannot disappear in the dilated weak limit, including at the specified endpoint cases or an infinite dilated horizon.
- **claim-dilated-core-kernel-majorization** (bound, [source](#stmt-nc-core-kernels)): There are nonnegative velocity measures $A_n(t,x,\,\mathrm dp)$ and numbers $\varepsilon_n\to0$ with the following properties. After discarding finitely many terms so that $r_n\le\overline T$, the measures $A_n$ are defined from free transport on the whole interval $0\le t\le1$, independently of the cap shot’s existence interval. The bounds on $A_n$ below hold on that whole interval; comparison with $h_n$ is used only while the shot exists.* 1. *On $0<t<\min(1,U_n)$, along almost every free characteristic, $$\label{nc:positive-equation} D_th_n\le 2P(A_n,h_n)+\varepsilon_n H .$$ At each point $A_n$ contains at most one main free core. Its dilated scale $\mu=s/r_n$ satisfies $\|y\|\asymp\mu$, with coordinates measured from its periodic copy, and $$\frac{2P(A_n,H)}{H}\le\frac{C}{t+\ell_n}.$$* 2. *Before spatial masks are imposed, a core of scale $\mu=s/r_n$ and sign $\sigma$ is the pushforward of $$\label{nc:core-parameters} \frac{K S\bigl(\gamma(z-tp_3)/\mu\bigr)} {\mu+t\alpha\gamma S\bigl(\gamma(z-tp_3)/\mu\bigr)} J(w)\,\mathrm dw , \qquad \bar p=d_s\bar w,\quad p_3=\sigma+\alpha\tanh\bigl(\gamma(z-tp_3)/\mu\bigr)+d_sw_3 .$$ The actual masks are measurable factors between zero and one. The density in <a href="#nc:core-parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="nc:core-parameters">[nc:core-parameters]</a> is at most $C(t+\mu)^{-1}J(w)$.* 3. *For $t\ge a>0$, the normalized gain measure $$\label{nc:normalized-kernel} \mathcal K_n(t,x,v;\,\mathrm dp\,\,\mathrm d\xi) = 4A_n(t,x,\,\mathrm dp)\, \delta\bigl((v-p)\cdot(\xi-v)\bigr) \frac{H(\xi)}{H(v)}\,\mathrm d\xi$$ has total mass at most $C/a$. Uniformly in its base point, the contributions from large $\|p\|+\|\xi-v\|$, or from large retained Gaussian parameters in a product-sheet majorant, tend to zero. Its total mass tends uniformly to zero as $\|v\|\to\infty$. The axial interval, axial mismatch, and small transverse input conclusions of Lemma <a href="#lem:kernel-tightness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:kernel-tightness">7</a> apply to $\mathcal K_n$, with the amplitude factor $C/a$.
  - Explanation: Free cores defined independently of the shot lifetime majorize hot growth while the shot exists. Their complete normalized kernels retain uniform tails and axial estimates on positive-time slabs.
- **claim-gain-endpoint-tightness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-nc-endpoint-tightness)): Fix $a>0$, and let $t_n\in[a,1]$. In a five-fold integral $(\mathcal V_n^5F_n)(t_n,x_n,v_n)$, assume $0\le F_n\le2$. For every prescribed error, one may restrict all $\|p_i\|+\|v_i-v_{i-1}\|$ and sheet parameters to fixed bounded sets at that error, uniformly in $n,t_n,x_n,v_n$. Moreover:* 1. *if $\|v_n\|\to\infty$, the whole integral tends to zero;* 2. *after retaining bounded endpoint velocities, an endpoint escaping every bounded neighborhood of every dilated periodic origin also has a vanishing integral.* *Consequently any sequence along which the five-fold integral has a positive lower bound has, after periodic translations and subselection, convergent endpoint positions and velocities.
  - Explanation: A positive iterated-gain contribution forces bounded velocities and positions relative to a dilated periodic origin. Normalized parameter and increment tails can be removed uniformly.
- **claim-axial-characteristic-matching** (bound, [source](#stmt-nc-characteristic-matching)): On every compact set with $t\ge a>0$, the following statements hold uniformly.* 1. *If $a\le t_2\le t_1$, $\Delta=t_1-t_2$, and $z_2=z_1-\Delta w$, then $$\label{nc:matching-inequality} \|w-u_{\mu,\sigma}(t_1,z_1)\| \le \|w-u_{\mu,\sigma}(t_2,z_2)\|.$$* 2. *As $\mu\downarrow0$, the distance of $u_{\mu,\sigma}(t,z)$ to $$\{z/t,\ \sigma-\alpha,\ \sigma+\alpha\}$$ tends to zero uniformly. For the perturbed axial velocity in <a href="#nc:core-parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="nc:core-parameters">[nc:core-parameters]</a>, $$\label{nc:thermal-speed-error} \|p_3-u_{\mu,\sigma}(t,z)\|\le d_s\|w_3\|.$$
  - Explanation: Axial profiles contract velocity mismatch along matching flights. At small scales their speeds approach the stated finite collection, and thermal perturbations have a uniform displacement bound.
- **claim-two-axial-gains-vanish** (theorem, [source](#stmt-nc-two-axial-gains)): Fix $a>0$. Let $t_n\in[a,1]$, and suppose the endpoint positions and velocities are bounded with $\bar v_n\to0$. Then $$(\mathcal V_n^2 1)(t_n,x_n,v_n)\longrightarrow0.$$ The conclusion continues to hold if all subsequent factors in a longer finite gain integral are bounded uniformly.
  - Explanation: At bounded endpoints approaching the velocity axis, successive gains vanish through non-atomic axial input and characteristic mismatch.
- **claim-redundant-plane-parameterization** (construction, [source](#stmt-nc-plane-parameters)): Let $n\ne0$, $e=n/\|n\|$, and let $\zeta$ be a bounded nonnegative function supported in $[-1,1]$ with integral one. For every nonnegative measurable $F$, $$\label{nc:plane-identity} \int_{n^\perp} F(k)\frac{\,\mathrm dA(k)}{\|n\|} = \int_{\mathbb R^3}F(n\times L)\|n\|\zeta(L\cdot e)\,\mathrm dL .$$ If $\delta\le\|n\|\le R$ and $\|k\|\le R_1$, the right side is supported in $$\|L\|\le\sqrt{(R_1/\delta)^2+1}$$ and has density at most $R\\|\zeta\\|_\infty$. The identity can be applied successively when each normal is a measurable function of parameters chosen at earlier gains.
  - Explanation: An auxiliary normal coordinate converts plane area into a volume parameter integral, with bounded support and density after normal and increment truncation.
- **claim-uniform-submersion-pullback** (theorem, [source](#stmt-nc-uniform-pullback)): Let $\mathcal O\subset\mathbb R^k$ be open, $k\ge d$, and suppose $\Phi_n,\Phi:\mathcal O\to\mathbb R^d$ satisfy $\Phi_n\to\Phi$ in $C^1_{\mathrm{loc}}$ and $\operatorname{rank}D\Phi=d$ almost everywhere. Let $K\Subset\mathcal O$ and $B\subset\mathbb R^d$ be compact. Let $a_n$ be measurable, supported in $K$, with $\|a_n\|\le A$. Let $F_n$ be measurable, supported in $B$, with $\|F_n\|\le M$, and converging to zero in measure. Then $$\label{nc:pullback-conclusion} \int_{\mathcal O}\|F_n(\Phi_n(q))a_n(q)\|\,\mathrm dq\longrightarrow0 .$$ No convergence or continuity of the densities $a_n$ is required.
  - Explanation: Almost-everywhere full rank and local differentiable convergence make bounded parameter densities uniformly insensitive to target functions vanishing in measure.
- **claim-five-gain-full-rank** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-five-gain-rank)): Fix a nonaxial endpoint velocity $v_0$, an endpoint $(t_0,x_0)$ with $t_0>0$, five positive scales $\mu_i$, and five signs $\sigma_i$. Given positive times $t_i$ and $L_i\in\mathbb R^3$, define recursively $$\label{nc:rank-map} \begin{split} x_i&=x_{i-1}-(t_{i-1}-t_i)v_{i-1},\\ p_i&=u_{\mu_i,\sigma_i}(t_i,z_i)e_3,\qquad n_i=v_{i-1}-p_i,\\ k_i&=n_i\times L_i,\qquad v_i=v_{i-1}+k_i , \qquad i=1,\ldots,5 . \end{split}$$ The map $$\Phi:(t_1,\ldots,t_5,L_1,\ldots,L_5) \longmapsto(t_5,x_5,v_5)$$ is real analytic on the connected open set $(0,\infty)^5\times\mathbb R^{15}$, and has rank seven almost everywhere. In particular it is a submersion almost everywhere on the ordered region $0<t_5<\cdots<t_1<t_0$.* *For convergent endpoint sequences with a nonaxial limiting velocity, convergent positive scale sequences, and the finite-temperature core formulas <a href="#nc:core-parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="nc:core-parameters">[nc:core-parameters]</a>, the analogous maps converge in $C^1$ on compact parameter sets to $\Phi$. Bounded measurable parameter densities supported in a common compact set therefore satisfy Lemma <a href="#nc:uniform-pullback" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="nc:uniform-pullback">27</a>, including after any measurable spatial, velocity, or time restrictions.
  - Explanation: The iterated collision map is analytic and has full target rank almost everywhere. Finite-temperature maps converge differentiably, allowing measurable masks in the pullback estimate.
- **claim-five-nonaxial-gains-vanish** (theorem, [source](#stmt-nc-five-nonaxial-gains)): Suppose $\ell_n\to0$, the hit endpoints converge after periodic translation, and their limiting velocity is nonaxial. Suppose also that $0\le f_n\le2$ on $a\le t\le\tau_n$ and $f_n\to0$ in measure on every compact subset of $$(0,U)\times\mathbb R^3_x \times\{v\in\mathbb R^3:\bar v\ne0\}.$$ Then $$(\mathcal V_n^5f_n)(\tau_n,x_n,v_n)\longrightarrow0.$$
  - Explanation: At a nonaxial limiting endpoint, finite gain regularization turns local convergence in measure into a vanishing iterated gain contribution.
- **claim-finite-dilated-horizon** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-finite-dilated-horizon)): There are fixed positive choices of $\eta,\alpha,\gamma,K$, with $\eta,\alpha,\gamma$ small and $K$ large, for which every limit in Propositions <a href="#prop:blowup-limit" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:blowup-limit">20</a> and <a href="#prop:nonzero-limit" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:nonzero-limit">21</a> has $U<\infty$. These parameters can be fixed before the final choices of $s_0,d_0,T_0$. The same choices work for every limiting mesh and every $l\in[0,1]$.
  - Explanation: Construction parameters can be fixed uniformly over limiting meshes so that a nonzero capped hot limit never has an infinite dilated lifespan.
- **claim-positive-history-lower-bound** (theorem, [source](#stmt-br-positive-histories)): Suppose $f\ge0$ solves $D_tf=2P(\widetilde B,f)-\nu f$ in the local transport sense. On bounded positive-time regions where the coefficients and collision terms are integrable, start with any nonnegative portion of $f(a)$. Integration of any collection of bounded finite histories with weights <a href="#br:history-weight" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="br:history-weight">[br:history-weight]</a> gives a measure dominated by $f(b)$.
  - Explanation: Positive finite collision histories generate measures bounded above by the solution at the terminal time.
- **claim-reference-retention-and-potential** (bound, [source](#stmt-br-retention)): For every $v\in E$, $p(v)\ge25/26$. The reference history sum restricted to velocities in $E$ is represented by a Markov flight with velocity jump rate $\lambda(v)=2p(v)\nu_0(v)$, jump law $J(v,\,\mathrm dv')\mathbf 1_E(v')/p(v)$, and multiplicative weight $$\exp\left(\int_0^A \mathcal W(V_s)\,\mathrm ds\right), \qquad \mathcal W(v)=(2p(v)-1)\nu_0(v).$$ One may take $$\label{br:potential-bounds} 0<c_0=\frac{48\pi}{13}\le \mathcal W(v),\qquad \lambda(v)\le C_0=48\pi\quad(v\in E).$$
  - Explanation: Velocity retention gives a Markov representation of the restricted branching histories with a uniformly positive potential and bounded jump rate.
- **claim-reference-volume-minorization** (bound, [source](#stmt-br-minorization)): There are $r_0>0$ and $\varepsilon_0>0$ such that $$\label{br:doeblin} P_1(v,A)\ge\varepsilon_0\|A\cap B(0,r_0)\|\quad(v\in E)$$ for every Borel set $A\subset E$. For each $\zeta>0$, there is also $\varepsilon_\zeta>0$ such that $$P_1(v,B(e_1,\zeta)\cap\{\|v'\|<D\})\ge\varepsilon_\zeta\quad(v\in E).$$
  - Explanation: Selected collisions produce a common volume component in every transition law and a uniform positive chance to reach the indicated interior velocity neighborhood.
- **claim-reference-mixing-and-displacement** (bound, [source](#stmt-br-mixing)): The reference process has a stationary probability law $\pi$ with $\int v\,\mathrm d\pi(v)=0$. There are $C,c>0$ such that $$\sup_{v\in E}\\|P_t(v,\cdot)-\pi\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\le Ce^{-ct}, \qquad \sup_{v\in E}\mathbb E_v\left\| A^{-1}\int_0^A V_s\,\mathrm ds\right\|^2\le\frac C A\quad(A\ge1).$$
  - Explanation: The confined reference flight has a mean-zero stationary law, exponential mixing, and a decreasing mean-square time-averaged velocity bound.
- **claim-reference-drift-growth-block** (construction, [source](#stmt-br-reference-block)): There is a finite $L$ such that, for all $$A\in I_L:=\left[\frac L{1+d},\frac L{1-d}\right],\qquad v\in E,$$ reference branching histories with velocities in $E$, final velocity strictly inside $E$, and displacement $Z_A$ satisfying $$\label{br:strict-drift} \|Z_A-\kappa Ae_1\|<\frac34d\kappa A$$ have total weight greater than 8.
  - Explanation: Reference histories can amplify mass while producing a controlled displacement and keeping their final velocities strictly inside the retained ball.
- **claim-measurable-medium-robust-growth** (construction, [source](#stmt-br-robust-block)): For these fixed $\kappa,L$, there are $J\in\mathbb N$ and $\varepsilon_*\in(0,1/4)$ with the following property. Suppose a measurable medium obeys, throughout every speed-$D$ excursion from a specified starting point over a duration $A\in I_L$, $$\label{br:medium-closeness} \|b_\sigma-1\|\le\varepsilon_*, \qquad \|w_\sigma-\sigma e_3\|\le\varepsilon_* \quad(\sigma=\pm1).$$ From each starting velocity in $E$, histories with at most $J$ collisions, speed at most $D$ on every free flight, every postcollision velocity and the final velocity strictly inside $E$, and $$\label{br:robust-drift} \|Z_A-\kappa Ae_1\|<d\kappa A$$ have total weight greater than 3. The initial free flight may have speed $D$. No continuity of the medium is needed.
  - Explanation: A bounded number of histories retains amplification and controlled displacement under sufficiently small measurable perturbations of the constant medium.
- **claim-accessibility-of-late-moving-boxes** (theorem, [source](#stmt-br-accessibility)): For the fixed parameters above, let $g\not\equiv0$ solve <a href="#eq:limit-equation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:limit-equation">[eq:limit-equation]</a> on $0<t<\infty$ with $0\le g\le(t+l)H$, for any allowed mesh and $l\in[0,1]$. Then $\int_{\mathcal B_t}g(t,x,v)\,\mathrm dx\,\mathrm dv>0$ at arbitrarily large finite times.
  - Explanation: Any nonzero eternal capped solution would place positive mass into the expanding moving boxes at arbitrarily late finite times.
- **claim-dormant-dilation-vanishes** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sep-dormant-vanishing)): As $T\downarrow0$, $$T^{-1}F^{\mathrm d}(Tt,Tx,v)\longrightarrow0$$ in distributions on compact subsets of $$0<t<1,\qquad x\in\mathbb R^3,\quad v\in\mathbb R^3,\quad \bar v\ne0 .$$ The evaluation in $x$ uses the periodic lift centered at the origin.
  - Explanation: The dormant solution vanishes under the shrinking time-space dilation away from the velocity axis, enabling comparison with the nonzero cap limit.

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-kernel-tightness",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $L<\\infty$. The following conclusions hold uniformly for $c\\in\\mathcal S_L$ and for outgoing velocities off the axis.*\n\n\n\n1.  *The tails in $|p|+|\\xi-v|$ are uniformly negligible: $$\\label{ker:uniform-tail}\n     \\lim_{R\\to\\infty}\\sup_{c\\in\\mathcal S_L}\\mathop{\\mathrm{ess\\,sup}}_v\n     \\mathcal K_c(v;|p|+|\\xi-v|>R)=0.$$ Also, $$\\label{ker:large-output}\n     \\lim_{M\\to\\infty}\\sup_{c\\in\\mathcal S_L}\n     \\mathop{\\mathrm{ess\\,sup}}_{|v|>M}\\mathcal K_c(v;\\mathbb R^3\\times\\mathbb R^3)=0.$$*\n\n\n\n2.  *For every fixed $M,\\delta>0$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n     &\\lim_{\\varepsilon\\downarrow0}\n     \\sup_{c\\in\\mathcal S_L}\n     \\mathop{\\mathrm{ess\\,sup}}_{\\substack{|v|\\leq M\\\\0<|\\bar v|\\leq\\varepsilon}}\n     \\mathcal K_c(v;|\\bar\\xi|\\geq\\delta)=0,\n     \\label{ker:axis-input}\\\\\n     &\\lim_{\\varepsilon\\downarrow0}\n     \\sup_{c\\in\\mathcal S_L}\n     \\mathop{\\mathrm{ess\\,sup}}_{\\substack{|v|\\leq M\\\\0<|\\bar v|\\leq\\varepsilon}}\n     \\mathcal K_c(v;|v_3-p_3|\\geq\\delta)=0.\n     \\label{ker:axis-mismatch}\n    \\end{aligned}$$*\n\n\n\n3.  *The axial input marginals are uniformly non-atomic. More precisely, for each $R\\geq1$ and each interval $I$ of length $0<\\ell\\leq1$, $$\\label{ker:axial-interval}\n     \\mathcal K_c(v;|p|+|\\xi-v|\\leq R,\\ \\xi_3\\in I)\n     \\leq C_{q,m,L,R}\\bigl(\\ell+\\ell^{2-q}\\bigr).$$ In combination with <a href=\"#ker:uniform-tail\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ker:uniform-tail\">[ker:uniform-tail]</a>, this gives $$\\label{ker:axial-modulus}\n     \\lim_{\\ell\\downarrow0}\\sup_{c\\in\\mathcal S_L}\\mathop{\\mathrm{ess\\,sup}}_v\n     \\sup_{|I|\\leq\\ell}\\mathcal K_c(v;\\xi_3\\in I)=0.$$*\n\n\n4.  *Let $\\mathcal S_L(\\varepsilon)$ denote the subclass whose elementary transverse widths are at most $\\varepsilon$. For every $M<\\infty$ and $r_0>0$, $$\\label{ker:off-axis-small-input}\n     \\lim_{\\delta\\downarrow0}\\limsup_{\\varepsilon\\downarrow0}\n     \\sup_{c\\in\\mathcal S_L(\\varepsilon)}\n     \\mathop{\\mathrm{ess\\,sup}}_{\\substack{|v|\\leq M\\\\|\\bar v|\\geq r_0}}\n     \\mathcal K_c(v;|\\bar\\xi|<\\delta)=0.$$*\n\n*All these conclusions hold under uniformly bounded sheet amplitudes.",
        "source_id": "PM-363.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ker-parameter-sheets",
        "exact_quote": "Let a measure in parameters $w=(\\bar w,w_3)\\in\\mathbb R^2\\times\\mathbb R$ have density $j$ satisfying $$0\\leq j(w)\\leq C_0\\pi^{-3/2}e^{-|w|^2}.$$ Let its velocity image be given by $$\\label{ker:parameter-map}\n \\bar p=d\\bar w,\\qquad p_3=\\Phi(w_3),\\qquad\n 0\\leq d\\leq\\tfrac1{16},\\qquad\n |\\Phi(a)|\\leq A+\\delta|a|,\\quad0\\leq\\delta\\leq\\tfrac1{16},$$ where $\\Phi$ is merely measurable. The image measure is dominated by $C_0$ times a sheet in $\\mathcal S_{L_A}$, with $L_A$ depending only on $A$. Its gain satisfies Lemma <a href=\"#lem:sheet-bound\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:sheet-bound\">6</a> with a constant depending only on $q,m,A,C_0$.*\n\n\n*If $c_{>B}$ is the image of the restricted parameter measure $\\mathbf 1_{|w|>B}j(w)\\,\\,\\mathrm dw$, then $$\\label{ker:parameter-tail}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{ess\\,sup}}_v\\frac{P(c_{>B},H)(v)}{H(v)}\n \\leq C_{q,m,A,C_0}e^{-B^2/2},\\qquad B\\geq0.$$ Thus parameter truncations are valid in the normalized collision measure, even when $\\Phi$ compresses intervals or has atoms in its image law. Probability mixtures of measurable families with common $q,m,A,C_0$ and the width bounds in <a href=\"#ker:parameter-map\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ker:parameter-map\">[ker:parameter-map]</a> preserve these assertions. More generally, a positive mixing measure of total mass $B_0<\\infty$ multiplies the domination amplitude and quantitative upper bounds by $B_0$; the sheet parameter $L_A$ is unchanged. For <a href=\"#ker:parameter-tail\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ker:parameter-tail\">[ker:parameter-tail]</a>, impose the restriction $|w|>B$ in each component before mixing. Additional measurable masks taking values in $[0,1]$ preserve the assertions.",
        "source_id": "PM-363.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-datum",
        "exact_quote": "The datum <a href=\"#eq:datum\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:datum\">[eq:datum]</a> satisfies $$\\int_\\Omega F_0(1+|v|^2+|\\log F_0|)\\,\\,\\mathrm dx\\,\\,\\mathrm dv<\\infty .$$ Its truncations $F_0^{(N)}$, retaining $j\\le N$, converge to $F_0$ in mass, energy, and relative entropy. In addition, if $\\chi\\ge0$ is a fixed bounded smooth compactly supported function of $(x,v)$, periodized after replacing $x$ by $x/s_N$, and $0\\le\\lambda_N\\le L_0<\\infty$, then $$F_0^{(N)}+\\lambda_Ns_N\\chi(x/s_N,v)$$ has the same limiting mass, energy, and relative entropy as $F_0$. For the last assertion $s_0$ may be decreased so that distinct periodic copies of the spatial support of the seed are disjoint.",
        "source_id": "PM-363.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-jet-barrier",
        "exact_quote": "The constant $C_m$ can be chosen so that $f_i\\le m_i$ on $[0,\\overline T]$, uniformly in the label. Moreover, almost everywhere along every free characteristic, $$D_tm_i\\ge\n \\left(1+\\tfrac78 |w_i|^2/d_{s_j}^2\\right)m_i .\n \\label{jet:barrier-derivative}$$ There is a constant $C_\\nu$, independent of the label, such that $$P(m_i,m_i)=m_i\\nu_{m_i},\\qquad\n \\nu_{m_i}(v)\\le C_\\nu\\frac K{s_j}(d_{s_j}+|w_i|).\n \\label{jet:barrier-collision}$$ Every free core remains in its own spatial enlargement: $$f_i(t,x,v)>0\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad x\\in\\mathcal O_i(t).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-363.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-jet-barrier",
          "exact_quote": "The constant $C_m$ can be chosen so that $f_i\\le m_i$ on $[0,\\overline T]$, uniformly in the label.           "source_id": "PM-363.2"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-cold-control",
        "exact_quote": "There is a constant $L$, depending on the fixed parameters, such that after imposing the additional condition $$Ld_0\\overline s^9\\le1,\n \\label{jet:thermal-comparison}$$ the following statements hold at every time of existence of the finite approximation in $0\\le t\\le\\overline T$.*\n\n\n1.  *For every retained label $i=(j,\\sigma,n)$, $$0\\le c_i\\le f_i+\\varepsilon_j m_i,\\qquad\n      \\varepsilon_j=Ld_{s_j}/s_j .\n      \\label{jet:cold-remainder}$$*\n\n\n2.  *At each spatial point call the unique label with $x\\in\\mathcal O_i(t)$, if one exists and is retained, the main label. Let $c_{\\mathrm{main}}$ be that color, or zero, and set $e=c-c_{\\mathrm{main}}$. For every $A\\ge0$, $$0\\le e(t,x,v)\\le C_A t^A e^{-4|v|^2}.\n      \\label{jet:escaped}$$ The upper bound remains valid after restricting the escaped sum to any subset of its labels.*\n\n\n3.  *If the main label has scale $s$, its velocity measure is bounded by a fixed multiple of $(t+s)^{-1}$ times a sum of admissible probability sheets from Lemma <a href=\"#lem:sheet-bound\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:sheet-bound\">6</a>. Its free-core contribution has the exact parameterization <a href=\"#eq:core-jacobian\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:core-jacobian\">[eq:core-jacobian]</a> below. The remainder has the smaller velocity-mass coefficient $Cd_s/s^2$. In particular, for every fixed $k\\ge0$, $$\\int_{\\mathbb R^3}\\langle v\\rangle^k c_{\\mathrm{main}}\\,\\,\\mathrm dv\n           \\le\\frac{C_k}{t+s},\\qquad\n      \\int_{|v|>R}\\langle v\\rangle^k c_{\\mathrm{main}}\\,\\,\\mathrm dv\n           \\le\\frac{C_k e^{-c_kR^2}}{t+s}\\quad(R\\ge3).\n      \\label{jet:moment-envelope}$$*\n\n\n4.  *For the mixed-cold source in the hot equation, $$\\sum_{\\substack{i,j\\in\\mathcal I_N\\\\i\\ne j}}\n           P^b(c_i,c_j)(t,x,v)\n           \\le C_A t^{A-1}H(v)\\qquad (t>0)\n      \\label{jet:mixed-source}$$ for every $A\\ge0$, with constants uniform in $N$ and $b$.",
        "source_id": "PM-363.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-jet-survival",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose a finite approximation satisfies $$0\\le h(t,x,v)\\le(t+s_N)H(v)$$ on an initial interval $[0,T]\\subset[0,\\overline T]$. All conclusions below concern times $t\\in[0,T]$. For every $M\\ge0$ there are constants $C,C_M$, independent of $T,N$, the cutoff, and the seed, such that $$\\begin{gathered}\n e^{-E_{s,N}(t)}f_i(t,x,v)\n       \\le c_i(t,x,v)\n       \\le f_i(t,x,v)+L(d_s/s)m_i(t,x,v),\\\\\n E_{s,N}(t)=C(d_st/s+t^2+s_Nt)+C_Mt^{M+1}\n\\end{gathered}\n \\label{jet:survival-sandwich}$$ for every label of scale $s$. Consequently, if $r\\to0$, $s_N\\le r$, $s/r\\in[\\epsilon,R]$ for fixed $0<\\epsilon<R<\\infty$, and $0\\le t\\le\\min(T,Ar)$, then $$E_{s,N}(t)\\le\n C_{\\epsilon,R,A}(d_s+r^2)+C_{M,A}r^{M+1}\\longrightarrow0$$ uniformly in the label and in points of its free core. The transverse free displacement divided by the stripe period satisfies $$\\frac{td_s}{s^2}\\le\n       C_{\\epsilon,R,A}r^9\\longrightarrow0.\n \\label{jet:stripe-displacement}$$ On these scales the difference between cold and free-core velocity measures also satisfies, uniformly in position and time, $$r\\int_{\\mathbb R^3}|c_i-f_i|(t,x,v)\\,\\,\\mathrm dv\\longrightarrow0.\n \\label{jet:survival-measure}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-363.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-jet-survival",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose a finite approximation satisfies $$0\\le h(t,x,v)\\le(t+s_N)H(v)$$ on an initial interval $[0,T]\\subset[0,\\overline T]$. Consequently, if $r\\to0$, $s_N\\le r$, $s/r\\in[\\epsilon,R]$ for fixed $0<\\epsilon<R<\\infty$, and $0\\le t\\le\\min(T,Ar)$, then $$E_{s,N}(t)\\le\n C_{\\epsilon,R,A}(d_s+r^2)+C_{M,A}r^{M+1}\\longrightarrow0$$ uniformly in the label and in points of its free core. The transverse free displacement divided by the stripe period satisfies $$\\frac{td_s}{s^2}\\le\n       C_{\\epsilon,R,A}r^9\\longrightarrow0.\n \\label{jet:stripe-displacement}$$ On these scales the difference between cold and free-core velocity measures also satisfies, uniformly in position and time, $$r\\int_{\\mathbb R^3}|c_i-f_i|(t,x,v)\\,\\,\\mathrm dv\\longrightarrow0.\n \\label{jet:survival-measure}$$",

The transverse free displacement divided by the stripe period satisfies $$\\frac{td_s}{s^2}\\le\n       C_{\\epsilon,R,A}r^9\\longrightarrow0.\n \\label{jet:stripe-displacement}$$ On these scales the difference between cold and free-core velocity measures also satisfies, uniformly in position and time, $$r\\int_{\\mathbb R^3}|c_i-f_i|(t,x,v)\\,\\,\\mathrm dv\\longrightarrow0.\n \\label{jet:survival-measure}$$",

    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-finite-cap",
        "exact_quote": "There is a fixed $T_0>0$ such that the following statements hold.*\n\n1.  *With $\\lambda=0$, the finite approximations exist on $[0,T_0]$ and satisfy $0\\le h\\le t^2H$.*\n\n\n2.  *There is a fixed finite $L_0$ such that, for every $0<T\\le T_0$ and every $N$, some $\\lambda\\in[0,L_0]$ gives an approximation on $[0,T]$ with $$\\label{eq:cap}\n    0\\le h(t,x,v)\\le(t+s_N)H(v),\\qquad\n    \\max_{0\\le t\\le T}\\frac{\\|h(t)\\|_H}{t+s_N}=1.$$*\n\n*In both cases all cold components obey Proposition <a href=\"#prop:cold-control\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:cold-control\">14</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-363.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-finite-cap",
          "exact_quote": "There is a fixed $T_0>0$ such that the following statements hold.*\n\n1.  *With $\\lambda=0$, the finite approximations exist on $[0,T_0]$ and satisfy $0\\le h\\le t^2H$.*\n\n\n2.  *There is a fixed finite $L_0$ such that, for every $0<T\\le T_0$ and every $N$, some $\\lambda\\in[0,L_0]$ gives an approximation on $[0,T]$ with $$\\label{eq:cap}\n    0\\le h(t,x,v)\\le(t+s_N)H(v),\\qquad\n    \\max_{0\\le t\\le T}\\frac{\\|h(t)\\|_H}{t+s_N}=1.$$*\n\n*In both cases all cold components obey Proposition <a href=\"#prop:cold-control\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:cold-control\">14</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-363.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There is a fixed $T_0>0$ such that the following statements hold.*\n\n1.  *With $\\lambda=0$, the finite approximations exist on $[0,T_0]$ and satisfy $0\\le h\\le t^2H$.*\n\n\n2.  *There is a fixed finite $L_0$ such that, for every $0<T\\le T_0$ and every $N$, some $\\lambda\\in[0,L_0]$ gives an approximation on $[0,T]$ with $$\\label{eq:cap}\n    0\\le h(t,x,v)\\le(t+s_N)H(v),\\qquad\n    \\max_{0\\le t\\le T}\\frac{\\|h(t)\\|_H}{t+s_N}=1.$$*\n\n*In both cases all cold components obey Proposition <a href=\"#prop:cold-control\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:cold-control\">14</a>.",
      "explanation": "The zero-seed family has a dormant upper bound. A bounded seed parameter can also be selected to attain the linear hot cap on each allowed observation interval.",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-finite-cap",

      "id": "claim-dormant-and-cap-approximations",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-finite-cap",
          "exact_quote": "There is a fixed finite $L_0$ such that, for every $0<T\\le T_0$ and every $N$, some $\\lambda\\in[0,L_0]$ gives an approximation on $[0,T]$ with $$\\label{eq:cap}\n    0\\le h(t,x,v)\\le(t+s_N)H(v),\\qquad\n    \\max_{0\\le t\\le T}\\frac{\\|h(t)\\|_H}{t+s_N}=1.$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-admissible-limits",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $T\\in(0,T_0]$. Either the zero-seed approximations of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:finite-cap\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:finite-cap\">16</a>, or any sequence of its cap shots on $[0,T]$, has a subsequence whose total densities $F_N=c_N+h_N$ converge weakly in $L^1([0,T]\\times\\Omega)$ to a nonnegative density $F$. The subsequence can be chosen so that $F_N(t)\\rightharpoonup F(t)$ in $L^1(\\Omega)$ at every $t\\in[0,T]$, with uniformly small polynomially weighted velocity tails. The limit has the common initial datum <a href=\"#eq:datum\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:datum\">[eq:datum]</a>, and $$Q^\\pm(F,F)\\in L^1([0,T]\\times\\Omega),\\qquad\nF\\in C([0,T];L^1(\\Omega)).$$ It satisfies every condition of Definition <a href=\"#def:admissible\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:admissible\">2</a> on this interval, including the inequalities at every time. Each such limit has an admissible global continuation. The dormant upper bound, consisting of the cold majorants of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:cold-control\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:cold-control\">14</a> and $t^2H$, passes to a zero-seed limit.",
        "source_id": "PM-363.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-admissible-limits",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $T\\in(0,T_0]$. The subsequence can be chosen so that $F_N(t)\\rightharpoonup F(t)$ in $L^1(\\Omega)$ at every $t\\in[0,T]$, with uniformly small polynomially weighted velocity tails. The limit has the common initial datum <a href=\"#eq:datum\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:datum\">[eq:datum]</a>, and $$Q^\\pm(F,F)\\in L^1([0,T]\\times\\Omega),\\qquad\nF\\in C([0,T];L^1(\\Omega)).$$ It satisfies every condition of Definition <a href=\"#def:admissible\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:admissible\">2</a> on this interval, including the inequalities at every time. Each such limit has an admissible global continuation. The dormant upper bound, consisting of the cold majorants of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:cold-control\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:cold-control\">14</a> and $t^2H$, passes to a zero-seed limit.",
          "source_id": "PM-363.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix $T\\in(0,T_0]$. Either the zero-seed approximations of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:finite-cap\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:finite-cap\">16</a>, or any sequence of its cap shots on $[0,T]$, has a subsequence whose total densities $F_N=c_N+h_N$ converge weakly in $L^1([0,T]\\times\\Omega)$ to a nonnegative density $F$. The subsequence can be chosen so that $F_N(t)\\rightharpoonup F(t)$ in $L^1(\\Omega)$ at every $t\\in[0,T]$, with uniformly small polynomially weighted velocity tails. The limit has the common initial datum <a href=\"#eq:datum\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:datum\">[eq:datum]</a>, and $$Q^\\pm(F,F)\\in L^1([0,T]\\times\\Omega),\\qquad\nF\\in C([0,T];L^1(\\Omega)).$$ It satisfies every condition of Definition <a href=\"#def:admissible\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:admissible\">2</a> on this interval, including the inequalities at every time. Each such limit has an admissible global continuation. The dormant upper bound, consisting of the cold majorants of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:cold-control\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:cold-control\">14</a> and $t^2H$, passes to a zero-seed limit.",
      "explanation": "At a fixed physical horizon, both approximation families admit admissible limits with the same datum, strong initial continuity, integrable collision terms, and admissible global continuations.",

        "anchor": "stmt-comp-averaging",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that $f_n$ and $g_n$ are locally uniformly integrable in $L^1$ on an open time-space-velocity region, and $(\\partial_t+v\\cdot\\nabla_x)f_n=g_n$ there in distributions.         "anchor": "stmt-prop-blowup-limit",
        "exact_quote": "Every sequence <a href=\"#eq:rescaling\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:rescaling\">[eq:rescaling]</a> has a subsequence for which $h_{r_N}\\rightharpoonup g$ in local spacetime $L^1$ on $(0,U)\\times\\mathbb R^3_x\\times\\mathbb R^3_v$, where $$\\label{eq:limit-equation}\n0\\leq g(t,x,v)\\leq(t+l)H(v),\\qquad\nD_tg=2P(B,g)-g\\nu_B.$$ Here $B$ is <a href=\"#eq:background\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:background\">[eq:background]</a>; its gain and loss are defined by integration against the velocity measure. Both terms on the right are locally integrable, with a velocity majorant uniform in position on each compact positive-time interval. Compactly supported velocity averages of $h_{r_N}$ converge strongly in local $L^1_{t,x}$. The limit has a locally strongly continuous $L^1$ representative at positive times and obeys the integrating-factor formula <a href=\"#dil:mild\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"dil:mild\">[dil:mild]</a> below.*\n\n\n*If $l>0$, pass also to a limit $\\lambda_N\\to\\lambda_0$ of the shot parameters.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-nc-core-kernels",
        "exact_quote": "There are nonnegative velocity measures $A_n(t,x,\\,\\mathrm dp)$ and numbers $\\varepsilon_n\\to0$ with the following properties. After discarding finitely many terms so that $r_n\\le\\overline T$, the measures $A_n$ are defined from free transport on the whole interval $0\\le t\\le1$, independently of the cap shot’s existence interval. The bounds on $A_n$ below hold on that whole interval; comparison with $h_n$ is used only while the shot exists.*\n\n\n1.  *On $0<t<\\min(1,U_n)$, along almost every free characteristic, $$\\label{nc:positive-equation}\n     D_th_n\\le 2P(A_n,h_n)+\\varepsilon_n H .$$ At each point $A_n$ contains at most one main free core. Its dilated scale $\\mu=s/r_n$ satisfies $|y|\\asymp\\mu$, with coordinates measured from its periodic copy, and $$\\frac{2P(A_n,H)}{H}\\le\\frac{C}{t+\\ell_n}.$$*\n\n\n2.  *Before spatial masks are imposed, a core of scale $\\mu=s/r_n$ and sign $\\sigma$ is the pushforward of $$\\label{nc:core-parameters}\n     \\frac{K S\\bigl(\\gamma(z-tp_3)/\\mu\\bigr)}\n          {\\mu+t\\alpha\\gamma S\\bigl(\\gamma(z-tp_3)/\\mu\\bigr)}\n     J(w)\\,\\mathrm dw ,\n     \\qquad\n     \\bar p=d_s\\bar w,\\quad\n     p_3=\\sigma+\\alpha\\tanh\\bigl(\\gamma(z-tp_3)/\\mu\\bigr)+d_sw_3 .$$ The actual masks are measurable factors between zero and one. The density in <a href=\"#nc:core-parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"nc:core-parameters\">[nc:core-parameters]</a> is at most $C(t+\\mu)^{-1}J(w)$.*\n\n\n3.  *For $t\\ge a>0$, the normalized gain measure $$\\label{nc:normalized-kernel}\n     \\mathcal K_n(t,x,v;\\,\\mathrm dp\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\xi)\n     =\n     4A_n(t,x,\\,\\mathrm dp)\\,\n     \\delta\\bigl((v-p)\\cdot(\\xi-v)\\bigr)\n     \\frac{H(\\xi)}{H(v)}\\,\\mathrm d\\xi$$ has total mass at most $C/a$. Uniformly in its base point, the contributions from large $|p|+|\\xi-v|$, or from large retained Gaussian parameters in a product-sheet majorant, tend to zero. Its total mass tends uniformly to zero as $|v|\\to\\infty$. The axial interval, axial mismatch, and small transverse input conclusions of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:kernel-tightness\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:kernel-tightness\">7</a> apply to $\\mathcal K_n$, with the amplitude factor $C/a$.",
        "source_id": "PM-363.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-nc-endpoint-tightness",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $a>0$, and let $t_n\\in[a,1]$. In a five-fold integral $(\\mathcal V_n^5F_n)(t_n,x_n,v_n)$, assume $0\\le F_n\\le2$. For every prescribed error, one may restrict all $|p_i|+|v_i-v_{i-1}|$ and sheet parameters to fixed bounded sets at that error, uniformly in $n,t_n,x_n,v_n$. Moreover:*\n\n1.  *if $|v_n|\\to\\infty$, the whole integral tends to zero;*\n\n2.  *after retaining bounded endpoint velocities, an endpoint escaping every bounded neighborhood of every dilated periodic origin also has a vanishing integral.*\n\n*Consequently any sequence along which the five-fold integral has a positive lower bound has, after periodic translations and subselection, convergent endpoint positions and velocities.",
        "source_id": "PM-363.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-nc-endpoint-tightness",

      "exact_statement": "Fix $a>0$, and let $t_n\\in[a,1]$. In a five-fold integral $(\\mathcal V_n^5F_n)(t_n,x_n,v_n)$, assume $0\\le F_n\\le2$.       "id": "claim-gain-endpoint-tightness",
      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-nc-endpoint-tightness",
          "exact_quote": "For every prescribed error, one may restrict all $|p_i|+|v_i-v_{i-1}|$ and sheet parameters to fixed bounded sets at that error, uniformly in $n,t_n,x_n,v_n$.",
          "source_id": "PM-363.2"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-nc-endpoint-tightness",
          "exact_quote": "Consequently any sequence along which the five-fold integral has a positive lower bound has, after periodic translations and subselection, convergent endpoint positions and velocities.",
          "source_id": "PM-363.2"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-nc-endpoint-tightness"
    },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-nc-characteristic-matching",

        "anchor": "stmt-nc-plane-parameters",
        "exact_quote": "Let $n\\ne0$, $e=n/|n|$, and let $\\zeta$ be a bounded nonnegative function supported in $[-1,1]$ with integral one.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-nc-uniform-pullback",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal O\\subset\\mathbb R^k$ be open, $k\\ge d$, and suppose $\\Phi_n,\\Phi:\\mathcal O\\to\\mathbb R^d$ satisfy $\\Phi_n\\to\\Phi$ in $C^1_{\\mathrm{loc}}$ and $\\operatorname{rank}D\\Phi=d$ almost everywhere. Let $K\\Subset\\mathcal O$ and $B\\subset\\mathbb R^d$ be compact. Let $a_n$ be measurable, supported in $K$, with $|a_n|\\le A$. Let $F_n$ be measurable, supported in $B$, with $|F_n|\\le M$, and converging to zero in measure. Then $$\\label{nc:pullback-conclusion}\n \\int_{\\mathcal O}|F_n(\\Phi_n(q))a_n(q)|\\,\\mathrm dq\\longrightarrow0 .$$ No convergence or continuity of the densities $a_n$ is required.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-five-gain-rank",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a nonaxial endpoint velocity $v_0$, an endpoint $(t_0,x_0)$ with $t_0>0$, five positive scales $\\mu_i$, and five signs $\\sigma_i$. Given positive times $t_i$ and $L_i\\in\\mathbb R^3$, define recursively $$\\label{nc:rank-map}\n \\begin{split}\n x_i&=x_{i-1}-(t_{i-1}-t_i)v_{i-1},\\\\\n p_i&=u_{\\mu_i,\\sigma_i}(t_i,z_i)e_3,\\qquad\n n_i=v_{i-1}-p_i,\\\\\n k_i&=n_i\\times L_i,\\qquad v_i=v_{i-1}+k_i ,\n \\qquad i=1,\\ldots,5 .\n \\end{split}$$ The map $$\\Phi:(t_1,\\ldots,t_5,L_1,\\ldots,L_5)\n       \\longmapsto(t_5,x_5,v_5)$$ is real analytic on the connected open set $(0,\\infty)^5\\times\\mathbb R^{15}$, and has rank seven almost everywhere. In particular it is a submersion almost everywhere on the ordered region $0<t_5<\\cdots<t_1<t_0$.*\n\n*For convergent endpoint sequences with a nonaxial limiting velocity, convergent positive scale sequences, and the finite-temperature core formulas <a href=\"#nc:core-parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"nc:core-parameters\">[nc:core-parameters]</a>, the analogous maps converge in $C^1$ on compact parameter sets to $\\Phi$. Bounded measurable parameter densities supported in a common compact set therefore satisfy Lemma <a href=\"#nc:uniform-pullback\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"nc:uniform-pullback\">27</a>, including after any measurable spatial, velocity, or time restrictions.",
        "source_id": "PM-363.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-five-gain-rank",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a nonaxial endpoint velocity $v_0$, an endpoint $(t_0,x_0)$ with $t_0>0$, five positive scales $\\mu_i$, and five signs $\\sigma_i$. Given positive times $t_i$ and $L_i\\in\\mathbb R^3$, define recursively $$\\label{nc:rank-map}\n \\begin{split}\n x_i&=x_{i-1}-(t_{i-1}-t_i)v_{i-1},\\\\\n p_i&=u_{\\mu_i,\\sigma_i}(t_i,z_i)e_3,\\qquad\n n_i=v_{i-1}-p_i,\\\\\n k_i&=n_i\\times L_i,\\qquad v_i=v_{i-1}+k_i ,\n \\qquad i=1,\\ldots,5 .\n \\end{split}$$ The map $$\\Phi:(t_1,\\ldots,t_5,L_1,\\ldots,L_5)\n       \\longmapsto(t_5,x_5,v_5)$$ is real analytic on the connected open set $(0,\\infty)^5\\times\\mathbb R^{15}$, and has rank seven almost everywhere. In particular it is a submersion almost everywhere on the ordered region $0<t_5<\\cdots<t_1<t_0$.*\n\n*For convergent endpoint sequences with a nonaxial limiting velocity, convergent positive scale sequences, and the finite-temperature core formulas <a href=\"#nc:core-parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"nc:core-parameters\">[nc:core-parameters]</a>, the analogous maps converge in $C^1$ on compact parameter sets to $\\Phi$. Bounded measurable parameter densities supported in a common compact set therefore satisfy Lemma <a href=\"#nc:uniform-pullback\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"nc:uniform-pullback\">27</a>, including after any measurable spatial, velocity, or time restrictions.",
          "source_id": "PM-363.2"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Fix a nonaxial endpoint velocity $v_0$, an endpoint $(t_0,x_0)$ with $t_0>0$, five positive scales $\\mu_i$, and five signs $\\sigma_i$. In particular it is a submersion almost everywhere on the ordered region $0<t_5<\\cdots<t_1<t_0$.*\n\n*For convergent endpoint sequences with a nonaxial limiting velocity, convergent positive scale sequences, and the finite-temperature core formulas <a href=\"#nc:core-parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"nc:core-parameters\">[nc:core-parameters]</a>, the analogous maps converge in $C^1$ on compact parameter sets to $\\Phi$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Admissible entropy-class solution** ([source](#stmt-def-admissible)): For a nonnegative datum $F_0$ satisfying $$\int_\Omega F_0(1+\|v\|^2+\|\log F_0\|)\,\mathrm dx\,\mathrm dv<\infty,$$ an admissible global solution is a nonnegative $F$ with the following properties. 1. $F\in C_w([0,\infty);L^1(\Omega))$, with $F(0)=F_0$, where weak continuity means continuity against every bounded measurable test. On each bounded time interval, $\int_\Omega F(1+\|v\|^2+\|\log F\|)$ is uniformly bounded. 2. $Q^\pm(F,F)/(1+G)$ belong to $L^1([0,T]\times\Omega)$ for every finite $T$. For every $\beta\in C^1([0,\infty))$ with $\beta(0)=0$ and $\|\beta'(z)\|\le C_\beta/(1+z)$, and every smooth test $\varphi(t,x,v)$ periodic in $x$ and compactly supported in $[0,\infty)\times\mathbb R^3_v$, $$\begin{split} &\int_0^\infty\!\!\int_\Omega \bigl[M\beta(G)(\partial_t\varphi+v\cdot\nabla_x\varphi) +\beta'(G)Q(F,F)\varphi\bigr]\,\mathrm dx\,\mathrm dv\,\mathrm dt\\ &\hspace{2cm} +\int_\Omega M\beta(F_0/M)\varphi(0,x,v)\,\mathrm dx\,\mathrm dv=0 . \end{split}$$ 3. With $\rho=\int F\,\mathrm dv$ and $j=\int vF\,\mathrm dv$, $\partial_t\rho+\nabla_x\cdot j=0$ distributionally with the initial density $\int F_0\,\mathrm dv$. Total momentum is conserved. For every $t\ge0$, $$\int_\Omega \|v\|^2F(t)\le\int_\Omega \|v\|^2F_0,\qquad \mathcal H_M(F(t))+\int_0^t\mathcal D(F(s))\,\mathrm ds \le\mathcal H_M(F_0).$$
- **Uniform sheet class** ([source](#stmt-ker-sheet-class)): Fix $L\geq1$. An elementary sheet in $\mathcal S_L$ is a product probability measure $$\label{ker:sheet-definition} c(\,\mathrm dp)=\mathcal G_d(\,\mathrm d\bar p)\rho(\,\mathrm dp_3),\qquad 0\leq d\leq\tfrac18,\qquad \int_{\mathbb R}e^{8a^2}\rho(\,\mathrm da)\leq L.$$ We also allow probability mixtures of elementary sheets. An upper bound by a sheet with amplitude $A_0$ means domination as measures by $A_0$ times such a mixture.
- **Singular velocity weight and weighted essential norm** ([source](#eq:weight)): Fix $1<q<2$ and $m=100$. The weight used throughout is $$\label{eq:weight} H(v)=e^{-\|v\|^2}\langle v\rangle^{-m} \bigl(1+\|\bar v\|^{-q}\bigr),\qquad \langle v\rangle=(1+\|v\|^2)^{1/2}.$$ Its values on the velocity axis can be assigned arbitrarily; all weighted supremum estimates are essential estimates. It is integrable with every polynomial velocity weight and lies in $L^p$ for some $p>1$. Write $\\|f\\|_H=\mathop{\mathrm{ess\,sup}}_{x,v}\|f(x,v)\|/H(v)$.
- **Collision gain and loss** ([source](#eq:collision)): For incoming velocities $v,v_*$ and $\omega\in S^2$, set $$a=(v-v_*)\cdot\omega,\qquad v'=v-a\omega,\qquad v_*'=v_*+a\omega.$$ The surface measure on $S^2$ is its ordinary area measure. We use $$\label{eq:collision} \begin{split} Q(F,F)&=Q^+(F,F)-Q^-(F,F),\\ Q^+(F,F)(v)&=\int_{\mathbb R^3\times S^2}\|a\|F(v')F(v_*')\,\mathrm dv_*\,\mathrm d\omega,\\ Q^-(F,F)(v)&=F(v)\nu_F(v). \end{split}$$ Arguments $t,x$, identical in every factor, are suppressed. The associated symmetric gain is denoted $P(f,g)$, so that $P(F,F)=Q^+(F,F)$. Its symmetry in the hard-sphere normalization is included in Lemma <a href="#lem:carleman" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:carleman">3</a>. The loss frequency is $$\label{eq:frequency} \nu_f(v)=2\pi\int_{\mathbb R^3}\|v-w\|f(w)\,\mathrm dw.$$ A superscript $b$ on $P,\nu,Q$ means that the same factor $\mathbf 1_{\{\|v-v_*\|<b\}}$ is inserted into gain and loss. This cutoff is invariant under elastic collision.
- **Collision loss frequency** ([source](#sec:framework)): The loss frequency is $$\label{eq:frequency} \nu_f(v)=2\pi\int_{\mathbb R^3}\|v-w\|f(w)\,\mathrm dw.$$
- **Cold and hot colored decomposition** ([source](#eq:colored)): The cold components are $c_i$, their sum is $c$, and $h$ denotes the hot component. Particles retain a cold label only in collisions within that label. All other collisions create hot particles. With $F=c+h$, this gives $$\label{eq:colored} \begin{split} (D_t)c_i&=P(c_i,c_i)-c_i\nu_F,\\ (D_t)h&=2P(c,h)+P(h,h)+\sum_{i\ne j}P(c_i,c_j)-h\nu_F. \end{split}$$ The sum over $i\ne j$ is ordered. Summing these equations gives $(D_t)F=Q(F,F)$. This identity also holds with a common collision cutoff. When labels include periodic copies, the individual functions are defined in the lift and are covariant under translations; their sums are periodic.
- **Complete normalized sheet kernel** ([source](#ker:normalized-kernel)): For such a sheet and $\bar v\ne0$, define its normalized kernel by $$\label{ker:normalized-kernel} \mathcal K_c(v;\,\mathrm dp\,\,\mathrm d\xi) =2\,c(\,\mathrm dp)\, \delta\bigl((v-p)\cdot(\xi-v)\bigr) \frac{H(\xi)}{H(v)}\,\,\mathrm d\xi.$$ The total mass is $P(c,H)(v)/H(v)$. Kernel restrictions always refer to this complete measure, including the ratio $H(\xi)/H(v)$.
- **Periodic multiscale cold-jet datum** ([source](#eq:datum)): The periodic datum is $$F_0(x,v)=\sum_{j\ge1}\sum_{\sigma=\pm1} \sum_{n\in\mathbb Z^3}c_{(j,\sigma,n)}^0(x,v). \label{eq:datum}$$
- **Implicit axial free-flow profile** ([source](#jet:scalar-flow)): For a fixed scale and sign, the equation $$u_s^\sigma(t,z)=a_s^\sigma(z-tu_s^\sigma(t,z)) \label{jet:scalar-flow}$$ defines $u_s^\sigma$ for all $t\ge0,z\in\mathbb R$.
- **Cap dilation** ([source](#eq:rescaling)): In the periodic lift about the origin define $$\label{eq:rescaling} \begin{gathered} r_N=t_{*,N}+s_N,\qquad h_{r_N}(t,x,v)=r_N^{-1}h_N(r_Nt,r_Nx,v),\qquad C_{r_N}(t,x,v)=r_Nc_N(r_Nt,r_Nx,v),\\ l_N=\frac{s_N}{r_N}\longrightarrow l\in[0,1],\qquad U_N=\frac{T_N}{r_N}\longrightarrow U\in[1,\infty]. \end{gathered}$$
- **Limiting two-sign axial background** ([source](#eq:background)): On an open mesh annulus $\mu<\|y\|<e^\eta\mu$, define the nonnegative velocity measure $$\label{eq:background} \begin{split} B(t,x,\,\mathrm dp) &=\sum_{\sigma=\pm1}b_{\mu,\sigma}(t,z) \delta_{u_{\mu,\sigma}(t,z)e_3}(\,\mathrm dp),\\ b_{\mu,\sigma}(t,z) &=\frac{\theta K S(\gamma(z-tu_{\mu,\sigma})/\mu)} {\mu+t\alpha\gamma S(\gamma(z-tu_{\mu,\sigma})/\mu)}. \end{split}$$
- **Backward Volterra gain operator** ([source](#nc:volterra)): $$\label{nc:volterra} (\mathcal V_n F)(t,x,v) = \int_a^t\int F\bigl(s,x-(t-s)v,\xi\bigr) \mathcal K_n\bigl(s,x-(t-s)v,v;\,\mathrm dp\,\,\mathrm d\xi\bigr)\,\mathrm ds .$$
- **Single-child collision sphere map** ([source](#br:sphere-map)): For $v,w\in\mathbb R^3$ and $n\in S^2$, set $$\label{br:sphere-map} \Phi(v,w,n)=\frac{v+w}{2}+\frac{\|v-w\|}{2}n, \qquad \,\mathrm d\varsigma(n)=\frac{\,\mathrm dn}{4\pi}.$$
- **Finite branching-history weight** ([source](#br:history-weight)): Its weight relative to $\prod_i\,\mathrm d\tau_i\,\mathrm d\varsigma(n_i)$ is $$\label{br:history-weight} \exp\left(-\int_a^b\nu(s,X_s,V_s)\,\mathrm ds\right) \prod_{i=1}^j2q_{\sigma_i}(\tau_i,X_{\tau_i},V_{\tau_i-}).$$

      "interpretation": "Stripe averaging produces a time-dependent measure supported at the transported axial speeds, with coefficients depending on the active mesh annulus.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Limiting two-sign axial background",
      "scope": "Dilated linear equation"
    },
    {
      "definition_anchor": "nc:volterra",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "nc:volterra",
          "exact_quote": "For a fixed $a>0$, define the backward Volterra operator $$\\label{nc:volterra}\n (\\mathcal V_n F)(t,x,v)\n =\n \\int_a^t\\int\n F\\bigl(s,x-(t-s)v,\\xi\\bigr)\n \\mathcal K_n\\bigl(s,x-(t-s)v,v;\\,\\mathrm dp\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\xi\\bigr)\\,\\mathrm ds .$$",
          "source_id": "PM-363.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "$$\\label{nc:volterra}\n (\\mathcal V_n F)(t,x,v)\n =\n \\int_a^t\\int\n F\\bigl(s,x-(t-s)v,\\xi\\bigr)\n \\mathcal K_n\\bigl(s,x-(t-s)v,v;\\,\\mathrm dp\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\xi\\bigr)\\,\\mathrm ds .$$",
      "interpretation": "The operator follows a backward free flight and integrates the complete normalized cold-hot gain.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Backward Volterra gain operator",
      "scope": "Nonconcentration on positive-time slabs"
    },
    {
      "definition_anchor": "br:sphere-map",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "br:sphere-map",
          "exact_quote": "For $v,w\\in\\mathbb R^3$ and $n\\in S^2$, set $$\\label{br:sphere-map}\n\\Phi(v,w,n)=\\frac{v+w}{2}+\\frac{|v-w|}{2}n,\n\\qquad \\,\\mathrm d\\varsigma(n)=\\frac{\\,\\mathrm dn}{4\\pi}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-363.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "For $v,w\\in\\mathbb R^3$ and $n\\in S^2$, set $$\\label{br:sphere-map}\n\\Phi(v,w,n)=\\frac{v+w}{2}+\\frac{|v-w|}{2}n,\n\\qquad \\,\\mathrm d\\varsigma(n)=\\frac{\\,\\mathrm dn}{4\\pi}.$$",
      "interpretation": "A uniform sphere direction generates the marginal law of either child of an elastic collision.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Single-child collision sphere map",
