<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-carleman-normalization** [claim](#stmt-lem-carleman) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-carleman): Push the collision-normal measure onto the outgoing sphere and apply coarea. -> Exact plane representation and extension to cold velocity measures
- **step-geometric-slices** [claim](#stmt-ker-geometric-slice) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ker-geometric-slice): Integrate the transverse singularity on lines, control axial intervals, and average over centered disks. -> Width-independent total and axial slice estimates
- **step-sheet-gain** [claim](#stmt-lem-sheet-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sheet-bound): Use collision energy to tilt the sheet, retain Gaussian tails, and sum dyadic shells. -> Uniform sheet gain and shell-tail bounds
- **step-kernel-tightness** [claim](#stmt-lem-kernel-tightness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-kernel-tightness): Remove shell tails and use complementary delta integrations near the axis and away from it. -> Complete normalized-kernel tightness and nonconcentration estimates
- **step-parameter-sheet-domination** [claim](#stmt-ker-parameter-sheets) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ker-parameter-sheets): Push forward product Gaussian majorants without differentiating the measurable axial map. -> Sheet domination and normalized parameter-tail control
- **step-hot-gain** [claim](#stmt-lem-hot-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hot-bound): Exploit incoming symmetry and a disk-mixture majorant, then sum the polynomially decreasing shell bounds. -> Quadratic gain bounded by the velocity majorant
- **step-disjoint-jet-geometry** [claim](#stmt-jet-geometry) / [proof](#proof-stmt-jet-geometry): Compare stripe gaps, radial margins, and axial intervals with their enlargements. -> At most one enlarged main label at a position
- **step-integrable-datum** [claim](#stmt-lem-datum) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-datum): Integrate each annular prototype and sum its mass, energy, and logarithmic bounds; estimate the seed overlap separately. -> Finite-entropy datum and common limiting initial entropy
- **step-maxwellian-barrier** [claim](#stmt-jet-barrier) / [proof](#proof-stmt-jet-barrier): Differentiate the implicit axial flow and the barrier along free paths, then use the Maxwellian collision identity. -> Free-core domination, positive transport derivative, and collision-frequency control
- **step-cold-control** [claim](#stmt-prop-cold-control) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-cold-control): Compare each color with free transport plus its barrier, sum escaped Gaussian tails, and compute the exact velocity Jacobian. -> Cold remainder, moment envelopes, and mixed-source control independent of the hot bound
- **step-core-survival** [claim](#stmt-jet-survival) / [proof](#proof-stmt-jet-survival): Bound the loss optical depth on core paths using same-color narrowness, escaped tails, and the hot cap. -> Asymptotic preservation of free cores and stripe masks under dilation
- **step-select-approximation-families** [claim](#stmt-prop-finite-cap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-finite-cap): Use local weighted mild theory, scalar supersolutions, and the boundary of the open strict-cap seed component. -> Dormant approximations and cap-attaining seeded approximations
- **step-velocity-averaging** [claim](#stmt-comp-averaging) / [proof](#proof-stmt-comp-averaging): Localize, invert damped transport, truncate the uniformly integrable source, and control Fourier high-frequency tails. -> Strong local compactness of velocity averages
- **step-fixed-horizon-admissibility** [claim](#stmt-prop-admissible-limits) / [proof](#pm-h-25): Combine annular uniform integrability with strong velocity averages to identify pair products; pass entropy at every time and concatenate a global continuation. -> Strongly continuous early admissible limits and global admissible continuations
- **step-dilated-linear-limit** [claim](#stmt-prop-blowup-limit) / [proof](#pm-h-30): Use core survival, the exact Jacobian, and stripe averaging for the cold weak limit; pair it with strongly convergent hot velocity averages. -> Linear hot equation in the time-dependent axial background
- **step-core-majorizing-kernels** [claim](#stmt-nc-core-kernels) / [proof](#proof-stmt-nc-core-kernels): Retain the main free core and absorb remainders, mixed-cold sources, and quadratic hot gain into a vanishing error. -> Positive majorizing equation and normalized core kernels
- **step-truncate-gain-endpoints** [claim](#stmt-nc-endpoint-tightness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-nc-endpoint-tightness): Remove normalized kernel tails and use small core amplitude at spatially escaping endpoints. -> Compact endpoint and path parameters modulo periodic translation
- **step-match-characteristic-speeds** [claim](#stmt-nc-characteristic-matching) / [proof](#proof-stmt-nc-characteristic-matching): Use the monotone implicit velocity equation and split profile values near endpoint speeds from the interior branch. -> Characteristic mismatch inequality and small-scale speed alternatives
- **step-axial-gain-vanishing** [claim](#stmt-nc-two-axial-gains) / [proof](#proof-stmt-nc-two-axial-gains): Remove small axial intervals around matching cold speeds and apply the second-kernel mismatch estimate to the remaining near-axis inputs. -> Vanishing successive gains at axial limiting endpoints
- **step-plane-volume-parameters** [claim](#stmt-nc-plane-parameters) / [proof](#proof-stmt-nc-plane-parameters): Add a redundant coordinate along the normal and use the area Jacobian of the cross-product map. -> Bounded volume parameter densities for truncated collision planes
- **step-uniform-pullback** [claim](#stmt-nc-uniform-pullback) / [proof](#proof-stmt-nc-uniform-pullback): Discard a small neighborhood of the critical set and use finitely many uniformly invertible coordinate charts. -> Vanishing pullbacks of bounded functions converging in measure
- **step-five-gain-rank** [claim](#stmt-lem-five-gain-rank) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-five-gain-rank): Exhibit a full-rank equal-time configuration, use analyticity to remove the critical set, and pass finite-temperature maps differentiably. -> Almost-everywhere submersion for the iterated gain map
- **step-nonaxial-gain-vanishing** [claim](#stmt-nc-five-nonaxial-gains) / [proof](#proof-stmt-nc-five-nonaxial-gains): Keep velocities away from the axis, remove tiny transverse positions and terminal-time layers, then apply the full-rank pullback estimate to finitely many scale tuples. -> Vanishing iterated gains at nonaxial hit endpoints
- **step-cap-limit-nonvanishing** [claim](#stmt-prop-nonzero-limit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-nc-five-nonaxial-gains-2): For a positive mesh endpoint retain the initial seed; otherwise iterate the positive inequality and contradict the cap using axial and nonaxial gain vanishing. -> Nonzero hot limit in the open dilated interval
- **step-positive-history-representation** [claim](#stmt-br-positive-histories) / [proof](#proof-stmt-br-positive-histories): Iterate the nonnegative gain in the free-path integrating-factor identity. -> Solution lower bounds from selected bounded finite histories
- **step-reference-retention** [claim](#stmt-br-retention) / [proof](#proof-stmt-br-retention): Compute the child squared-speed distribution and rewrite the retained history weights as a confined Markov flight with potential. -> Uniform retention, positive potential, and bounded jump rate
- **step-reference-minorization** [claim](#stmt-br-minorization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-br-minorization): Select angular patches in successive collisions with a full-rank final velocity map, and integrate over separated collision times. -> Common volume minorization and interior velocity access
- **step-reference-mixing** [claim](#stmt-br-mixing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-br-mixing): Contract total variation by the common transition mass, use velocity-negation symmetry, and integrate the decaying velocity correlations. -> Mean-zero stationary law, exponential mixing, and displacement control
- **step-reference-growth-block** [claim](#stmt-br-reference-block) / [proof](#proof-stmt-br-reference-block): Combine a low-displacement mixed flight, interior velocity access, and a final no-jump drift with the positive potential. -> Amplifying histories with strict prescribed displacement
- **step-robust-measurable-block** [claim](#stmt-br-robust-block) / [proof](#proof-stmt-br-robust-block): Retain finitely many compact reference tests with strict margins, cover starting states and durations, and perturb the finite path weights. -> Uniform finite-history amplification for measurable media
- **step-access-late-boxes** [claim](#stmt-br-accessibility) / [proof](#proof-stmt-br-accessibility): Freely transport positive nonaxial mass outside the mesh hole, select collisions leading to the desired drift velocity, then retain finite free-flight survival. -> Positive mass in moving boxes at late finite times
- **step-finite-horizon-growth-contradiction** [claim](#stmt-prop-finite-dilated-horizon) / [proof](#proof-stmt-br-accessibility-2): Choose construction parameters uniformly over meshes, repeat robust growth in expanding boxes, and contradict the polynomial cap mass bound. -> Finite dilated observation horizon
- **step-dormant-vanishing** [claim](#stmt-sep-dormant-vanishing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sep-dormant-vanishing): Dilate the dormant upper bound and use transverse Gaussian decay off the velocity axis. -> Vanishing dormant dilation on off-axis compact sets
- **step-separate-and-continue** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sep-dormant-vanishing-2): Assume equality along shrinking fixed horizons, choose diagonal approximants, and compare their dormant dilation with the nonzero cap limit at a comparable scale. -> Distinct early same-datum solutions and admissible global continuations

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-same-datum-nonuniqueness**: A nonnegative periodic datum with bounded velocity support and finite mass, energy, and absolute entropy admits distinct global admissible solutions. Both have strong continuity and integrable collision gains and losses on a common initial interval.
- **invariant-fixed-horizon-admissibility**: At a fixed physical horizon, dormant approximations and cap shots admit subsequential limits with the same initial datum. The limits satisfy the admissibility requirements at every time and admit global continuation.
- **invariant-velocity-average-compactness**: Local uniform integrability of a transported sequence and its source yields local strong compactness of compactly supported smooth velocity averages.
- **invariant-cap-limit-nonvanishing**: Every extracted dilated hot limit from the specified shrinking-horizon cap shots is nonzero on its open existence interval.
- **invariant-finite-dilated-horizon**: Fixed construction parameters force every extracted nonzero dilated limit to have finite horizon, uniformly over the allowed meshes and lower mesh endpoints.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-universal-datum-nonuniqueness**: Conclude that every finite-entropy periodic initial density has distinct admissible evolutions. -> An existential construction for a particular singular datum does not establish nonuniqueness for arbitrary data.
- **anti-homogeneous-transfer**: Apply the nonuniqueness conclusion to conservative spatially homogeneous hard-sphere evolution. -> The paper explicitly distinguishes homogeneous conservative uniqueness from its spatially inhomogeneous construction.
- **anti-extra-local-conservation**: Advertise the constructed solutions as satisfying exact local momentum and local energy conservation. -> The stated admissibility requirements impose local mass conservation and total momentum conservation, together with global inequalities.
- **anti-cap-hit-as-separation-certificate**: Treat a vanishing seed and an attained cap as sufficient evidence that the limiting solutions differ. -> A supremum event can carry vanishing mass and disappear under weak convergence.
- **anti-single-axial-gain-decay**: Assume that a normalized gain vanishes whenever the outgoing velocity approaches the axis. -> A matching axial cold atom produces a strictly positive limiting normalized gain.
- **anti-interchange-compactness-procedures**: Pass dilated cold–hot products using the fixed-horizon compactness argument for products of densities. -> Dilated cold profiles concentrate into velocity measures, changing the product convergence problem.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The theorem concerns continuum solutions obtained through subsequence limits, nonconcentration, a branching-growth contradiction, and global continuation. Proposition 18 states: “Each such limit has an admissible global continuation.” The proof of the main theorem concludes: “Thus for all sufficiently small horizons no cap limit can equal the fixed dormant limit.” These existence and separation arguments do not provide a finite input/output procedure whose execution certifies the theorem. Finite collision histories are analytical lower bounds within that continuum argument.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The same-datum nonuniqueness theorem limits uniqueness claims for continuum kinetic models but supplies no software implementation contract. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The collision representation concerns velocity densities and measures. It does not construct a discrete particle simulator or establish its convergence to the singular solutions. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Cap selection establishes existence of a suitable seed amplitude through continuous dependence and a boundary argument. It provides no terminating procedure for finding or certifying that amplitude. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The admissible-solution construction extracts weakly convergent subsequences without a computable extraction rule or convergence rate. It does not yield a finite algorithm for producing the distinct global solutions. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The sheet class specifies probability measures, mixtures, and an exponential moment condition. These are analytical objects without a finite representation or supported data-structure operations. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Uniform gain bounds control collision integrals as velocity widths shrink. They establish neither a discretization nor an accelerator mapping, arithmetic contract, or hardware resource bound. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The branching-history lower bound concerns a kinetic transport equation with collision gain and loss. It contains no adversarial model, security construction, or cryptographic guarantee. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The uniform pullback estimate assumes convergence in measure and almost-everywhere full rank for continuum maps. It does not construct a learning method or supply generalization guarantees for trained models. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The reference displacement block establishes existence of a duration supporting sufficient branching weight. It specifies no optimization objective, search algorithm, or certificate of optimality. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Robustness under measurable perturbations refers to collision densities and partner velocities along bounded flights. It does not model communication, scheduling, replicas, or distributed failures. |
| other (Kinetic theory and mathematical analysis) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The annular cold-jet datum is a concrete analytical construction with finite mass, energy, and absolute entropy. Together with the distinct admissible limits, it supplies a continuum counterexample against uniqueness criteria based solely on the stated admissibility conditions. Adapting it into a finite numerical benchmark requires additional approximation and selection analysis. |

On each bounded time interval, $\\int_\\Omega F(1+|v|^2+|\\log F|)$ is uniformly bounded.\n\n2.  $Q^\\pm(F,F)/(1+G)$ belong to $L^1([0,T]\\times\\Omega)$ for every finite $T$. <a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-existential-singular-datum** (counterexample_scope): The nonuniqueness conclusion concerns the constructed spatially concentrated datum; it does not establish nonuniqueness for arbitrary finite-entropy initial densities. Blocked use: ['Claiming distinct admissible evolutions for every finite-entropy datum.', 'Inferring that bounded velocity support also bounds the spatial amplitude.']
- **limit-conservation-contract** (formal_scope): Admissibility requires local mass conservation and total momentum conservation, together with global energy and entropy-dissipation inequalities. Exact local momentum and local energy conservation are not established as additional requirements of this class. Blocked use: ['Advertising this theorem as proving nonuniqueness within a class requiring exact local momentum and local energy conservation.']
- **limit-homogeneous-regime** (counterexample_scope): The spatially inhomogeneous construction does not transfer to the conservative homogeneous hard-sphere regime discussed in the paper. Blocked use: ['Using this counterexample to reject homogeneous conservative uniqueness.']
- **limit-stronger-uniqueness-regimes** (counterexample_scope): The result does not remove the additional hypotheses in the cited Maxwellian-bounded and perturbative uniqueness theories. Blocked use: ['Claiming nonuniqueness for the cited near-Maxwellian spatial Sobolev class.', 'Applying the singular construction as a counterexample within a uniform Maxwellian upper-bound class.']
- **limit-normalization-and-measure-gain** (hypothesis): The plane representation and outgoing collision measure use the paper's hard-sphere normalization. Extension to a cold velocity measure requires finiteness of the displayed gain almost everywhere. Blocked use: ['Transferring the gain coefficient to a different angular normalization without conversion.', 'Treating the measure-valued gain formula as finite for arbitrary input measures.']
- **limit-sheet-domination** (hypothesis): Uniform cold gain estimates require the stated sheet structure, exponential axial moment control, and bounded domination amplitudes. Axial regularity is not required, but arbitrary transverse laws are not covered. Blocked use: ['Applying width-independent sheet estimates to unrelated velocity measures.', 'Dropping the exponential moment condition while retaining the stated tail conclusions.']
- **limit-weight-essential-axis** (edge_case): Weighted supremum bounds are essential estimates. Assigned values directly on the velocity axis do not provide pointwise information there. Blocked use: ['Evaluating an essential weighted supremum as a pointwise certificate on the velocity axis.', 'Treating changes on the axis as changes to the represented density.']
- **limit-single-axial-gain** (edge_case): A normalized gain need not vanish as the outgoing velocity approaches the axis. The axial nonconcentration argument requires successive gains and characteristic matching. Blocked use: ['Replacing the axial successive-gain argument with decay of a single normalized gain.']
- **limit-cold-control-parameter-order** (hypothesis): Cold comparison uses the thermal smallness conditions after the fixed construction parameters have been chosen. Its bounds apply during existence of the finite approximation on the preliminary interval. Blocked use: ['Using the preliminary cold estimates beyond their stated interval.', 'Choosing thermal widths independently of the comparison constants.', 'Reading a conditional comparison estimate as an independent global existence theorem.']
- **limit-core-survival-full-cap** (hypothesis): Cold-core survival requires the hot cap on the entire preceding initial interval. The asymptotic comparison also restricts the cold scale to a fixed comparable range relative to the dilation scale. Blocked use: ['Deducing small optical depth from a cap verified only at the terminal time.', 'Claiming uniform free-core approximation simultaneously over unrestricted scale ratios.']
- **limit-cap-selection-not-finite-search** (nonconstructive): Cap selection uses the boundary of an open component of seed parameters and continuous dependence. The argument does not provide a certified terminating numerical procedure for selecting the cap parameter. Blocked use: ['Presenting cap selection as a finite seed-search algorithm with a certified stopping tolerance.']
- **limit-fixed-horizon-versus-dilation** (approximation): Fixed-horizon admissibility and shrinking-horizon dilation use different compactness arguments. Dilated cold profiles converge to velocity measures, so identifying their product with the hot density requires the separate mixed-product argument. Blocked use: ['Passing dilated cold–hot products solely by the fixed-horizon argument for products of densities.', 'Treating the limiting axial background as a stationary density.']
- **limit-positive-time-dilated-traces** (edge_case): The general dilated limit equation and local continuity are asserted at positive times. Extension to the initial trace requires a positive lower mesh endpoint and a convergent subsequence of seed parameters. Blocked use: ['Assigning the stated initial seed trace when the lower mesh endpoint vanishes.', 'Assuming continuity at the cap-event boundary follows from local weak convergence on the open interval.']
- **limit-cap-hit-not-separation** (approximation): An attained weighted cap and a vanishing seed alone do not establish separation of weak limits. Nonconcentration must preserve nonzero interior hot mass, and the branching argument must control the dilated horizon. Blocked use: ['Treating a finite-approximation supremum event as a proof that the limiting same-datum solutions differ.']
- **limit-full-rank-pullback** (hypothesis): The pullback estimate requires local differentiable convergence, almost-everywhere full target rank, common compact support, and uniform bounds on the parameter densities and target functions. The collision-rank application additionally fixes a nonaxial endpoint and positive limiting scales. Blocked use: ['Replacing differentiable convergence by sampled agreement of collision maps.', 'Applying the nonaxial rank argument directly at an axial endpoint.', 'Dropping compact truncations or uniform density bounds in the pullback step.']
- **limit-robust-history-block** (hypothesis): Robust branching amplification assumes density and partner-velocity closeness throughout every specified bounded-speed excursion. Measurability is allowed, but closeness only at selected collision points does not establish the full loss comparison. Blocked use: ['Inferring robust amplification from endpoint closeness alone.', 'Applying the block to an unrestricted medium without the excursion-wide bounds.']
- **limit-no-computable-separating-horizon** (nonconstructive): Separation uses a diagonal choice of approximants and a contradiction along shrinking horizons. No convergence rate uniform in the observation horizon is assumed, so the argument does not furnish a finite computational witness for the distinct global solutions. Blocked use: ['Claiming a computable separating horizon or a certified finite discretization from this existence proof.', 'Reporting a finite simulation as certification of the continuum theorem.']
- **limit-global-versus-early-regularity** (formal_scope): Strong continuity and unrenormalized collision integrability are guaranteed on a common initial interval. Global continuation preserves the stated admissibility class; it does not extend these stronger early-time conclusions to every later time. Blocked use: ['Advertising global integrability of the unrenormalized collision gains and losses solely from the main theorem.', 'Replacing global weak continuity by global strong continuity without further proof.']

### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 363 (`lean/docs/363.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Nonuniqueness for the periodic hard-sphere Boltzmann equation](../../preprints/Nonuniqueness-for-the-periodic-hard-sphere-Boltzmann-equation-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization proves nonuniqueness for the periodic hard-sphere Boltzmann equation by constructing two distinct global renormalized solutions on $\mathbb T^3\times\mathbb R^3$ with the same nonnegative initial density. The data have bounded velocity support and finite mass, energy, and absolute entropy. Both solutions conserve local mass and total momentum and satisfy the global energy and entropy-dissipation inequalities.
>
> On a common initial interval they are strongly continuous in $L^1$, with integrable collision gains and losses. These are the conditions of the selected periodic result; later local-conservation refinements are outside it.

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

/-!
# Nonuniqueness for the periodic hard-sphere Boltzmann equation

Theorem 1.1, with the full entropy-class admissibility of Definition 2.1 and
strong initial L¹ continuity with integrable collision gain and loss.
-/

noncomputable section

open MeasureTheory Set Filter
open scoped RealInnerProductSpace ENNReal Topology

namespace BoltzmannNonuniqueness

abbrev Velocity := EuclideanSpace ℝ (Fin 3)
abbrev Position := Fin 3 → AddCircle (1 : ℝ)
abbrev Phase := Position × Velocity
abbrev Density := Phase → ℝ
abbrev Sphere := Metric.sphere (0 : Velocity) 1

/-- Ordinary surface area, rather than normalized probability on the sphere. -/
def sphereArea : Measure Sphere := (volume : Measure Velocity).toSphere

def normalComponent (v w ω : Velocity) : ℝ := inner ℝ (v - w) ω

def scatterLeft (v w ω : Velocity) : Velocity := v - normalComponent v w ω • ω

def scatterRight (v w ω : Velocity) : Velocity := w + normalComponent v w ω • ω

def collisionKernel (v w ω : Velocity) : ℝ := |normalComponent v w ω|

def gain (f : Density) (x : Position) (v : Velocity) : ℝ :=
  ∫ p : Velocity × Sphere,
    collisionKernel v p.1 p.2 * f (x, scatterLeft v p.1 p.2) *
      f (x, scatterRight v p.1 p.2)
    ∂((volume : Measure Velocity).prod sphereArea)

def loss (f : Density) (x : Position) (v : Velocity) : ℝ :=
  ∫ p : Velocity × Sphere,
    collisionKernel v p.1 p.2 * f (x, v) * f (x, p.1)
    ∂((volume : Measure Velocity).prod sphereArea)

def collision (f : Density) (x : Position) (v : Velocity) : ℝ :=
  gain f x v - loss f x v

run_cmd Lean.modifyEnv fun env => Lean.Meta.auxLemmasExt.setState env {}

abbrev Evolution := ℝ → Density
abbrev SpaceTimePhase := ℝ × Phase
abbrev LiftedSpaceTimePhase := ℝ × (Velocity × Velocity)

def torusProjection (x : Velocity) : Position := fun i => (x i : AddCircle (1 : ℝ))

/-- A representative in the fundamental half-open unit cube, used only to evaluate periodic
derivatives. Periodicity makes those derivatives independent of this choice. -/
def torusRepresentative (x : Position) : Velocity :=
  WithLp.toLp 2 (fun i => ((AddCircle.equivIco (1 : ℝ) 0) (x i) : ℝ))

def maxwellian (v : Velocity) : ℝ :=
  (2 * Real.pi) ^ (-(3 / 2 : ℝ)) * Real.exp (-‖v‖ ^ 2 / 2)

def entropyMoment (f : Density) (z : Phase) : ℝ :=
  f z * (1 + ‖z.2‖ ^ 2 + |Real.log (f z)|)

/-- The convention `0 |log 0| = 0` is automatic in this real-valued moment. -/
def FiniteEntropyDatum (f : Density) : Prop :=
  (∀ᵐ z, 0 ≤ f z) ∧ AEStronglyMeasurable f ∧ Integrable (entropyMoment f)

def BoundedVelocitySupport (f : Density) : Prop :=
  ∃ R : ℝ, 0 < R ∧ ∀ᵐ z, R < ‖z.2‖ → f z = 0

def energy (f : Density) : ℝ := ∫ z, ‖z.2‖ ^ 2 * f z

def momentum (f : Density) : Velocity := ∫ z, f z • z.2

def relativeEntropy (f : Density) : ℝ :=
  ∫ z, f z * Real.log (f z / maxwellian z.2) - f z + maxwellian z.2

/-- Lower semicontinuous extension of `(A-B) log(A/B)` on the nonnegative quadrant.
The off-quadrant value is immaterial for nonnegative densities. -/
def extendedEntropyProduction (A B : ℝ) : ℝ≥0∞ :=
  if A = 0 ∧ B = 0 then 0
  else if 0 < A ∧ 0 < B then ENNReal.ofReal ((A - B) * Real.log (A / B))
  else ⊤

def entropyDissipation (f : Density) : ℝ≥0∞ :=
  (4 : ℝ≥0∞)⁻¹ * ∫⁻ p : Position × (Velocity × (Velocity × Sphere)),
    ENNReal.ofReal (collisionKernel p.2.1 p.2.2.1 p.2.2.2) *
      extendedEntropyProduction
        (f (p.1, scatterLeft p.2.1 p.2.2.1 p.2.2.2) *
          f (p.1, scatterRight p.2.1 p.2.2.1 p.2.2.2))
        (f (p.1, p.2.1) * f (p.1, p.2.2.1))
    ∂((volume : Measure Position).prod ((volume : Measure Velocity).prod
      ((volume : Measure Velocity).prod sphereArea)))

/-- The renormalizations of Definition 2.1, with the right derivative at zero. -/
structure Renormalization where
  toFun : ℝ → ℝ
  regular : ContDiffOn ℝ 1 toFun (Ici 0)
  at_zero : toFun 0 = 0
  derivative_bound : ∃ C : ℝ, 0 ≤ C ∧ ∀ z ∈ Ici (0 : ℝ),
    |derivWithin toFun (Ici 0) z| ≤ C / (1 + z)

/-- A smooth test, periodic in the spatial variable and compact in time and velocity. -/
structure KineticTest where
  toFun : ℝ → Position → Velocity → ℝ
  regular : ContDiff ℝ (⊤ : ℕ∞) (fun p : LiftedSpaceTimePhase =>
    toFun p.1 (torusProjection p.2.1) p.2.2)
  compact_time_velocity : ∃ R : ℝ, 0 < R ∧ ∀ t x v,
    R < |t| ∨ R < ‖v‖ → toFun t x v = 0

def KineticTest.transportDerivative (φ : KineticTest) (t : ℝ) (z : Phase) : ℝ :=
  fderiv ℝ (fun p : LiftedSpaceTimePhase => φ.toFun p.1 (torusProjection p.2.1) p.2.2)
    (t, torusRepresentative z.1, z.2) (1, z.2, 0)

/-- Smooth local mass tests have no velocity cutoff, since their velocity dependence is
introduced only by the transport derivative. -/
structure MassTest where
  toFun : ℝ → Position → ℝ
  regular : ContDiff ℝ (⊤ : ℕ∞) (fun p : ℝ × Velocity => toFun p.1 (torusProjection p.2))
  compact_time : ∃ R : ℝ, 0 < R ∧ ∀ t x, R < |t| → toFun t x = 0

def MassTest.transportDerivative (φ : MassTest) (t : ℝ) (z : Phase) : ℝ :=
  fderiv ℝ (fun p : ℝ × Velocity => φ.toFun p.1 (torusProjection p.2))
    (t, torusRepresentative z.1) (1, z.2)

def relativeDensity (F : Evolution) (t : ℝ) (z : Phase) : ℝ := F t z / maxwellian z.2

def renormalizedIntegrand (F : Evolution) (β : Renormalization) (φ : KineticTest)
    (p : SpaceTimePhase) : ℝ :=
  maxwellian p.2.2 * β.toFun (relativeDensity F p.1 p.2) *
      φ.transportDerivative p.1 p.2 +
    derivWithin β.toFun (Ici 0) (relativeDensity F p.1 p.2) *
      collision (F p.1) p.2.1 p.2.2 * φ.toFun p.1 p.2.1 p.2.2

def nonnegativeTimes : Set SpaceTimePhase := Ici (0 : ℝ) ×ˢ univ
def boundedTimes (T : ℝ) : Set SpaceTimePhase := Icc 0 T ×ˢ univ

/-- Full global admissibility, including both separately renormalized collision terms,
local mass, total momentum, and the every-time energy and entropy inequalities. -/
structure AdmissibleGlobal (f₀ : Density) (F : Evolution) : Prop where
  datum : FiniteEntropyDatum f₀
  spacetime_measurable : AEStronglyMeasurable (fun p : SpaceTimePhase => F p.1 p.2)
    (volume.restrict nonnegativeTimes)
  finite_each_time : ∀ t ∈ Ici (0 : ℝ), FiniteEntropyDatum (F t)
  initial : F 0 =ᵐ[volume] f₀
  weak_continuity : ∀ ψ : Phase → ℝ, Measurable ψ →
    (∃ C : ℝ, ∀ z, |ψ z| ≤ C) →
    ContinuousOn (fun t => ∫ z, F t z * ψ z) (Ici 0)
  uniform_moment : ∀ T : ℝ, 0 ≤ T → ∃ C : ℝ, ∀ t ∈ Icc 0 T,
    (∫ z, entropyMoment (F t) z) ≤ C
  -- These finiteness conditions prevent the real Bochner integrals defining `gain` and
  -- `loss` from silently taking the value zero at divergent collision fibers.
  collision_fibers : ∀ T : ℝ, 0 ≤ T → ∀ᵐ p ∂volume.restrict (boundedTimes T),
    Integrable (fun q : Velocity × Sphere =>
      collisionKernel p.2.2 q.1 q.2 *
        F p.1 (p.2.1, scatterLeft p.2.2 q.1 q.2) *
        F p.1 (p.2.1, scatterRight p.2.2 q.1 q.2))
      ((volume : Measure Velocity).prod sphereArea) ∧
    Integrable (fun q : Velocity × Sphere =>
      collisionKernel p.2.2 q.1 q.2 * F p.1 p.2 * F p.1 (p.2.1, q.1))
      ((volume : Measure Velocity).prod sphereArea)
  gain_integrable : ∀ T : ℝ, 0 ≤ T → IntegrableOn
    (fun p : SpaceTimePhase => gain (F p.1) p.2.1 p.2.2 /
      (1 + relativeDensity F p.1 p.2)) (boundedTimes T)
  loss_integrable : ∀ T : ℝ, 0 ≤ T → IntegrableOn
    (fun p : SpaceTimePhase => loss (F p.1) p.2.1 p.2.2 /
      (1 + relativeDensity F p.1 p.2)) (boundedTimes T)
  renormalized_integrable : ∀ β φ, IntegrableOn (renormalizedIntegrand F β φ)
    nonnegativeTimes
  renormalized_equation : ∀ β : Renormalization, ∀ φ : KineticTest,
    (∫ p in nonnegativeTimes, renormalizedIntegrand F β φ p) +
      (∫ z, maxwellian z.2 * β.toFun (f₀ z / maxwellian z.2) *
        φ.toFun 0 z.1 z.2) = 0
  local_mass_integrable : ∀ φ : MassTest, IntegrableOn
    (fun p : SpaceTimePhase => F p.1 p.2 * φ.transportDerivative p.1 p.2)
    nonnegativeTimes
  local_mass : ∀ φ : MassTest,
    (∫ p in nonnegativeTimes, F p.1 p.2 * φ.transportDerivative p.1 p.2) +
      (∫ z, f₀ z * φ.toFun 0 z.1) = 0
  momentum_conservation : ∀ t ∈ Ici (0 : ℝ), momentum (F t) = momentum f₀
  energy_inequality : ∀ t ∈ Ici (0 : ℝ), energy (F t) ≤ energy f₀
  entropy_inequality : ∀ t ∈ Ici (0 : ℝ),
    ENNReal.ofReal (relativeEntropy (F t)) +
      (∫⁻ s in Icc (0 : ℝ) t, entropyDissipation (F s)) ≤
        ENNReal.ofReal (relativeEntropy f₀)

/-- Additional regularity asserted on the common initial interval. The `eLpNorm` statement
is strong `L¹` continuity and cannot be made vacuous by a nonintegrable Bochner integral. -/
structure StrongEarly (T : ℝ) (F : Evolution) : Prop where
  positive_time : 0 < T
  strong_continuity : ∀ t ∈ Icc 0 T,
    Tendsto (fun s => eLpNorm (fun z => F s z - F t z) 1 volume)
      (𝓝[Icc 0 T] t) (𝓝 0)
  gain_integrable : IntegrableOn (fun p : SpaceTimePhase => gain (F p.1) p.2.1 p.2.2)
    (boundedTimes T)
  loss_integrable : IntegrableOn (fun p : SpaceTimePhase => loss (F p.1) p.2.1 p.2.2)
    (boundedTimes T)

theorem nonuniqueness :
    ∃ f₀ : Density, BoundedVelocitySupport f₀ ∧
      ∃ (F G : Evolution) (T : ℝ), AdmissibleGlobal f₀ F ∧ AdmissibleGlobal f₀ G ∧
        StrongEarly T F ∧ StrongEarly T G ∧
        ∃ t ∈ Set.Icc 0 T, ¬(F t =ᵐ[MeasureTheory.volume] G t) :=
  sorry

end BoltzmannNonuniqueness

end

end OAI
```

      "statement_lean": "abbrev Sphere := Metric.sphere (0 : Velocity) 1"

    {

      "kind": "def",
      "name": "sphereArea",
      "statement_lean": "def sphereArea : Measure Sphere := (volume : Measure Velocity).toSphere"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "normalComponent",
      "statement_lean": "def normalComponent (v w ω : Velocity) : ℝ := inner ℝ (v - w) ω"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "scatterLeft",
      "statement_lean": "def scatterLeft (v w ω : Velocity) : Velocity := v - normalComponent v w ω • ω"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "scatterRight",
      "statement_lean": "def scatterRight (v w ω : Velocity) : Velocity := w + normalComponent v w ω • ω"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "collisionKernel",
      "statement_lean": "def collisionKernel (v w ω : Velocity) : ℝ := |normalComponent v w ω|"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "gain",
      "statement_lean": "def gain (f : Density) (x : Position) (v : Velocity) : ℝ :=\n  ∫ p : Velocity × Sphere,\n    collisionKernel v p.1 p.2 * f (x, scatterLeft v p.1 p.2) *\n      f (x, scatterRight v p.1 p.2)\n    ∂((volume : Measure Velocity).prod sphereArea)"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "loss",
      "statement_lean": "def loss (f : Density) (x : Position) (v : Velocity) : ℝ :=\n  ∫ p : Velocity × Sphere,\n    collisionKernel v p.1 p.2 * f (x, v) * f (x, p.1)\n    ∂((volume : Measure Velocity).prod sphereArea)"
    },

      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",

    },

      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "torusProjection",
      "statement_lean": "def torusProjection (x : Velocity) : Position := fun i => (x i : AddCircle (1 : ℝ))"
    },
    {
      "docstring": "A representative in the fundamental half-open unit cube, used only to evaluate periodic\nderivatives. Periodicity makes those derivatives independent of this choice.",
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "torusRepresentative",
      "statement_lean": "def torusRepresentative (x : Position) : Velocity :=\n  WithLp.toLp 2 (fun i => ((AddCircle.equivIco (1 : ℝ) 0) (x i) : ℝ))"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "maxwellian",
      "statement_lean": "def maxwellian (v : Velocity) : ℝ :=\n  (2 * Real.pi) ^ (-(3 / 2 : ℝ)) * Real.exp (-‖v‖ ^ 2 / 2)"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "entropyMoment",
      "statement_lean": "def entropyMoment (f : Density) (z : Phase) : ℝ :=\n  f z * (1 + ‖z.2‖ ^ 2 + |Real.log (f z)|)"
    },
    {
      "docstring": "The convention `0 |log 0| = 0` is automatic in this real-valued moment.",
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "FiniteEntropyDatum",
      "statement_lean": "def FiniteEntropyDatum (f : Density) : Prop :=\n  (∀ᵐ z, 0 ≤ f z) ∧ AEStronglyMeasurable f ∧ Integrable (entropyMoment f)"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "BoundedVelocitySupport",
      "statement_lean": "def BoundedVelocitySupport (f : Density) : Prop :=\n  ∃ R : ℝ, 0 < R ∧ ∀ᵐ z, R < ‖z.2‖ → f z = 0"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "energy",
      "statement_lean": "def energy (f : Density) : ℝ := ∫ z, ‖z.2‖ ^ 2 * f z"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "momentum",
      "statement_lean": "def momentum (f : Density) : Velocity := ∫ z, f z • z.2"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "relativeEntropy",
      "statement_lean": "def relativeEntropy (f : Density) : ℝ :=\n  ∫ z, f z * Real.log (f z / maxwellian z.2) - f z + maxwellian z.2"
    },

      "docstring": "Lower semicontinuous extension of `(A-B) log(A/B)` on the nonnegative quadrant.\nThe off-quadrant value is immaterial for nonnegative densities.",
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "extendedEntropyProduction",
      "statement_lean": "def extendedEntropyProduction (A B : ℝ) : ℝ≥0∞ :=\n  if A = 0 ∧ B = 0 then 0\n  else if 0 < A ∧ 0 < B then ENNReal.ofReal ((A - B) * Real.log (A / B))\n  else ⊤"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "entropyDissipation",
      "statement_lean": "def entropyDissipation (f : Density) : ℝ≥0∞ :=\n  (4 : ℝ≥0∞)⁻¹ * ∫⁻ p : Position × (Velocity × (Velocity × Sphere)),\n    ENNReal.ofReal (collisionKernel p.2.1 p.2.2.1 p.2.2.2) *\n      extendedEntropyProduction\n        (f (p.1, scatterLeft p.2.1 p.2.2.1 p.2.2.2) *\n          f (p.1, scatterRight p.2.1 p.2.2.1 p.2.2.2))\n        (f (p.1, p.2.1) * f (p.1, p.2.2.1))\n    ∂((volume : Measure Position).prod ((volume : Measure Velocity).prod\n      ((volume : Measure Velocity).prod sphereArea)))"
    },

      "statement_lean": "structure Renormalization where\n  toFun : ℝ → ℝ\n  regular : ContDiffOn ℝ 1 toFun (Ici 0)\n  at_zero : toFun 0 = 0\n  derivative_bound : ∃ C : ℝ, 0 ≤ C ∧ ∀ z ∈ Ici (0 : ℝ),\n    |derivWithin toFun (Ici 0) z| ≤ C / (1 + z)"
    },
    {

      "kind": "structure",
      "name": "KineticTest",
      "statement_lean": "structure KineticTest where\n  toFun : ℝ → Position → Velocity → ℝ\n  regular : ContDiff ℝ (⊤ : ℕ∞) (fun p : LiftedSpaceTimePhase =>\n    toFun p.1 (torusProjection p.2.1) p.2.2)\n  compact_time_velocity : ∃ R : ℝ, 0 < R ∧ ∀ t x v,\n    R < |t| ∨ R < ‖v‖ → toFun t x v = 0"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "KineticTest.transportDerivative",
      "statement_lean": "def KineticTest.transportDerivative (φ : KineticTest) (t : ℝ) (z : Phase) : ℝ :=\n  fderiv ℝ (fun p : LiftedSpaceTimePhase => φ.toFun p.1 (torusProjection p.2.1) p.2.2)\n    (t, torusRepresentative z.1, z.2) (1, z.2, 0)"
    },

    },

    },
    {
      "docstring": null,

    },

      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "renormalizedIntegrand",
      "statement_lean": "def renormalizedIntegrand (F : Evolution) (β : Renormalization) (φ : KineticTest)\n    (p : SpaceTimePhase) : ℝ :=\n  maxwellian p.2.2 * β.toFun (relativeDensity F p.1 p.2) *\n      φ.transportDerivative p.1 p.2 +\n    derivWithin β.toFun (Ici 0) (relativeDensity F p.1 p.2) *\n      collision (F p.1) p.2.1 p.2.2 * φ.toFun p.1 p.2.1 p.2.2"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "nonnegativeTimes",

    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "boundedTimes",
      "statement_lean": "def boundedTimes (T : ℝ) : Set SpaceTimePhase := Icc 0 T ×ˢ univ"
    },

      "statement_lean": "structure AdmissibleGlobal (f₀ : Density) (F : Evolution) : Prop where\n  datum : FiniteEntropyDatum f₀\n  spacetime_measurable : AEStronglyMeasurable (fun p : SpaceTimePhase => F p.1 p.2)\n    (volume.restrict nonnegativeTimes)\n  finite_each_time : ∀ t ∈ Ici (0 : ℝ), FiniteEntropyDatum (F t)\n  initial : F 0 =ᵐ[volume] f₀\n  weak_continuity : ∀ ψ : Phase → ℝ, Measurable ψ →\n    (∃ C : ℝ, ∀ z, |ψ z| ≤ C) →\n    ContinuousOn (fun t => ∫ z, F t z * ψ z) (Ici 0)\n  uniform_moment : ∀ T : ℝ, 0 ≤ T → ∃ C : ℝ, ∀ t ∈ Icc 0 T,\n    (∫ z, entropyMoment (F t) z) ≤ C\n  -- These finiteness conditions prevent the real Bochner integrals defining `gain` and\n  -- `loss` from silently taking the value zero at divergent collision fibers.\n  collision_fibers : ∀ T : ℝ, 0 ≤ T → ∀ᵐ p ∂volume.restrict (boundedTimes T),\n    Integrable (fun q : Velocity × Sphere =>\n      collisionKernel p.2.2 q.1 q.2 *\n        F p.1 (p.2.1, scatterLeft p.2.2 q.1 q.2) *\n        F p.1 (p.2.1, scatterRight p.2.2 q.1 q.2))\n      ((volume : Measure Velocity).prod sphereArea) ∧\n    Integrable (fun q : Velocity × Sphere =>\n      collisionKernel p.2.2 q.1 q.2 * F p.1 p.2 * F p.1 (p.2.1, q.1))\n      ((volume : Measure Velocity).prod sphereArea)\n  gain_integrable : ∀ T : ℝ, 0 ≤ T → IntegrableOn\n    (fun p : SpaceTimePhase => gain (F p.1) p.2.1 p.2.2 /\n      (1 + relativeDensity F p.1 p.2)) (boundedTimes T)\n  loss_integrable : ∀ T : ℝ, 0 ≤ T → IntegrableOn\n    (fun p : SpaceTimePhase => loss (F p.1) p.2.1 p.2.2 /\n      (1 + relativeDensity F p.1 p.2)) (boundedTimes T)\n  renormalized_integrable : ∀ β φ, IntegrableOn (renormalizedIntegrand F β φ)\n    nonnegativeTimes\n  renormalized_equation : ∀ β : Renormalization, ∀ φ : KineticTest,\n    (∫ p in nonnegativeTimes, renormalizedIntegrand F β φ p) +\n      (∫ z, maxwellian z.2 * β.toFun (f₀ z / maxwellian z.2) *\n        φ.toFun 0 z.1 z.2) = 0\n  local_mass_integrable : ∀ φ : MassTest, IntegrableOn\n    (fun p : SpaceTimePhase => F p.1 p.2 * φ.transportDerivative p.1 p.2)\n    nonnegativeTimes\n  local_mass : ∀ φ : MassTest,\n    (∫ p in nonnegativeTimes, F p.1 p.2 * φ.transportDerivative p.1 p.2) +\n      (∫ z, f₀ z * φ.toFun 0 z.1) = 0\n  momentum_conservation : ∀ t ∈ Ici (0 : ℝ), momentum (F t) = momentum f₀\n  energy_inequality : ∀ t ∈ Ici (0 : ℝ), energy (F t) ≤ energy f₀\n  entropy_inequality : ∀ t ∈ Ici (0 : ℝ),\n    ENNReal.ofReal (relativeEntropy (F t)) +\n      (∫⁻ s in Icc (0 : ℝ) t, entropyDissipation (F s)) ≤\n        ENNReal.ofReal (relativeEntropy f₀)"
    },

      "statement_lean": "structure StrongEarly (T : ℝ) (F : Evolution) : Prop where\n  positive_time : 0 < T\n  strong_continuity : ∀ t ∈ Icc 0 T,\n    Tendsto (fun s => eLpNorm (fun z => F s z - F t z) 1 volume)\n      (𝓝[Icc 0 T] t) (𝓝 0)\n  gain_integrable : IntegrableOn (fun p : SpaceTimePhase => gain (F p.1) p.2.1 p.2.2)\n    (boundedTimes T)\n  loss_integrable : IntegrableOn (fun p : SpaceTimePhase => loss (F p.1) p.2.1 p.2.2)\n    (boundedTimes T)"
    },
    {

      "statement_lean": "theorem nonuniqueness :\n    ∃ f₀ : Density, BoundedVelocitySupport f₀ ∧\n      ∃ (F G : Evolution) (T : ℝ), AdmissibleGlobal f₀ F ∧ AdmissibleGlobal f₀ G ∧\n        StrongEarly T F ∧ StrongEarly T G ∧\n        ∃ t ∈ Set.Icc 0 T, ¬(F t =ᵐ[MeasureTheory.volume] G t) :=\n  sorry"

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove nonuniqueness for the periodic hard-sphere Boltzmann equation by constructing two distinct global renormalized solutions with the same nonnegative initial density on $`\mathbb T^3\times\mathbb R^3`$. This density has bounded velocity support and finite mass, energy, and absolute entropy. Both solutions conserve local mass and total momentum and satisfy the global energy and entropy-dissipation inequalities. On a common initial interval, they are strongly continuous in <i>L</i><sup>1</sup>, and their collision gains and losses are integrable.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

We study the Cauchy problem for the hard-sphere Boltzmann equation on $\Omega=\mathbb T^3_x\times\mathbb R^3_v$, where $\mathbb T^3=\mathbb R^3/\mathbb Z^3$. A nonnegative density $F(t,x,v)$ evolves by free transport and binary elastic collisions: $$(\partial_t+v\cdot\nabla_x)F=Q(F,F),\qquad F(0,x,v)=F_0(x,v).$$ For incoming velocities $v,v_*$ and a collision normal $\omega\in S^2$, put $$a=(v-v_*)\cdot\omega,\qquad
 v'=v-a\omega,\qquad v_*'=v_*+a\omega.$$ Our normalization is $$Q(F,F)(v)=\int_{\mathbb R^3\times S^2}|a|
       \bigl(F(v')F(v_*')-F(v)F(v_*)\bigr)\,\mathrm dv_*\,\mathrm d\omega,$$ with the common variables $t,x$ suppressed and ordinary surface area measure on $S^2$. The initial density is assumed to have finite mass, kinetic energy, and absolute entropy, meaning that $\int_\Omega F_0(1+|v|^2+|\log F_0|)<\infty$, with $0|\log0|=0$. The question is whether these physical bounds and the renormalized equation determine the subsequent evolution uniquely.

The global existence and weak stability theory of DiPerna and Lions provides solutions at this level of integrability (DiPerna and Lions 1989) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[DiPernaLions1989](#ref-DiPernaLions1989)]. Their entropy-production inequality explicitly includes periodic space (DiPerna and Lions 1991) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[DiPernaLions1991](#ref-DiPernaLions1991)] <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[LevermoreMasmoudi2010](#ref-LevermoreMasmoudi2010)]; (Levermore and Masmoudi 2010, Theorem 4.1) gives a periodic formulation with a Maxwellian reference density. The need for renormalization is substantive: the stated bounds do not themselves make the quadratic collision term locally integrable. Existence and weak stability do not supply uniqueness. The uniqueness problem for general renormalized solutions is discussed, for example, in (Silvestre 2022, sec. 2) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Silvestre2022](#ref-Silvestre2022)].

We use the following precise admissibility requirements, written in full in Definition <a href="#def:admissible" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:admissible">2</a>. With $M(v)=(2\pi)^{-3/2}e^{-|v|^2/2}$, the equation is tested through $M\beta(F/M)$ for every $C^1$ function $\beta$ satisfying $\beta(0)=0$ and $|\beta'(z)|\le C_\beta/(1+z)$; the separately renormalized gain and loss are integrable on every finite time interval. The solution is weakly continuous in $L^1(\Omega)$, against every bounded measurable test, and takes the prescribed initial value. Its mass, energy, and absolute entropy remain bounded on bounded time intervals. It conserves local mass and total momentum and satisfies both the global energy inequality and the relative-entropy inequality with the full Boltzmann entropy dissipation at every time. Here admissibility does not require local momentum or local energy conservation. Our main result concerns uniqueness within this class.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *There is a nonnegative density $F_0$ on $\mathbb T^3\times\mathbb R^3$, with bounded velocity support and $$\int_{\mathbb T^3\times\mathbb R^3}
 F_0(1+|v|^2+|\log F_0|)\,\mathrm dx\,\mathrm dv<\infty,$$ for which the hard-sphere Boltzmann equation has two distinct global admissible solutions, in the sense of Definition <a href="#def:admissible" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:admissible">2</a>, with initial datum $F_0$. On some common initial interval both solutions are strongly continuous in $L^1$, and their collision gains and losses are integrable in space-time-velocity.*

</div>

The two solutions therefore obey the unrenormalized equation on the interval where their difference is established. Their common datum has bounded velocities but unbounded amplitudes concentrated at successively smaller spatial scales. The conservation and dissipation requirements remain part of the conclusion.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Uniqueness and related constructions

Classical uniqueness theorems impose additional control, such as Maxwellian upper bounds (Kaniel and Shinbrot 1978) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[KanielShinbrot1978](#ref-KanielShinbrot1978)]. For periodic cutoff hard potentials, Ukai’s perturbative theory gives global existence and uniqueness near a Maxwellian in a velocity-weighted spatial Sobolev class, with mass, momentum, and energy matched to that Maxwellian (Ukai 1974, Theorem 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Ukai1974](#ref-Ukai1974)].

The distinction between spatially homogeneous and inhomogeneous evolution is also important. For the homogeneous hard-sphere equation, Mischler and Wennberg prove uniqueness in a conservative class from finite mass and energy alone (Mischler and Wennberg 1999, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[MischlerWennberg1999](#ref-MischlerWennberg1999)]. Wennberg’s homogeneous nonuniqueness examples for hard potentials permit increasing energy (Wennberg 1999) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Wennberg1999](#ref-Wennberg1999)]. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> instead concerns a spatially inhomogeneous datum, with the energy and entropy-dissipation inequalities retained.

Recent instability constructions give another relevant comparison. Chen and Holmer prove failure of uniform continuity in weighted Sobolev spaces for the constant collision kernel (Chen and Holmer 2024, Theorem 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[ChenHolmer2024](#ref-ChenHolmer2024)]; Chen, Guo, Shen, and Zhang establish failure of uniform continuity for the hard-sphere solution map on $\mathbb R^3_x\times\mathbb R^3_v$, in Gaussian-weighted $L^2_vH^s_x$ spaces for $0\le s<1$ (Chen et al. 2026, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[ChenGuoShenZhang2026](#ref-ChenGuoShenZhang2026)]. Those results compare solutions with arbitrarily close initial data. Here the two initial data agree exactly, on periodic space, and both resulting solutions satisfy the admissibility conditions above. The instability in (Chen et al. 2026) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[ChenGuoShenZhang2026](#ref-ChenGuoShenZhang2026)] is driven by collision loss and dispersion. In the construction below, collision gain amplifies a vanishing hot seed and produces a nonzero weak limit near the initial spatial singularity.

For the related Krieger–Strain model, Gismondi, Golding, and Novack construct rough stationary solutions in a mollifier-confluent sense: the collision operators of mollified densities tend to zero in distributions for every nonnegative Schwartz mollifier of mass one (Gismondi, Golding, and Novack 2026, Definition 1.3 and Theorem 1.5) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[GismondiGoldingNovack2026](#ref-GismondiGoldingNovack2026)]. They also conjecture nonuniqueness of renormalized inhomogeneous Landau and Boltzmann solutions for a dense class of initial data. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> concerns one periodic hard-sphere datum in the specified admissibility class.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### One datum and two approximation families

Write $x=(y,z)\in\mathbb R^2\times\mathbb R$ in a lift of the torus, and $v=(\bar v,v_3)\in\mathbb R^2\times\mathbb R$. The datum consists of narrow velocity distributions, called cold jets, near the axial directions $\pm e_3$. They occupy transverse annuli of geometrically decreasing radii $s_j\downarrow0$. Within each annulus the two velocity signs occupy disjoint, finely alternating stripes. Their thermal widths are much smaller than the stripe gaps. An expanding axial velocity profile bounds the spatial density of each freely transported jet at scale $s$ by $C/(t+s)$. Annular mass is $O(s^2)$, and annular absolute entropy is $O(s^2(1+|\log s|))$, so all scales together define a single finite-entropy datum.

Assign a color to each cold jet. A collision within one color retains that color; collisions between different colors, or with particles already declared hot, produce hot particles. This is an exact decomposition: the sum of the colored equations is the Boltzmann equation. The narrow velocity distributions suppress self-collisions, and the spatial gaps suppress collisions between distinct cold jets at early times.

Retain the first $N$ annuli and impose a collision cutoff that is removed as $N\to\infty$. Let $h_N$ denote the hot component. The estimates use an integrable velocity weight $H(v)$, Gaussian at infinity and locally comparable to $|\bar v|^{-q}$ near the velocity axis, with $1<q<2$. A *cap* is an almost-everywhere upper bound on $h_N/H$. The two approximation families have the following properties:

1.  With no hot particles initially, the hot density remains bounded by $t^2H(v)$. We call this the dormant family.

2.  For every sufficiently short observation horizon $T>0$, a smooth hot seed of vanishing mass, energy, and entropy can be selected so that $h_N\le(t+s_N)H$ on $[0,T]$, with equality in the weighted essential supremum at some time. Here $s_N$ is the smallest retained annular radius.

At each fixed horizon, uniform integrability of both the densities and their two-particle products gives admissible limits with exactly the same initial datum. A vanishing seed and a cap hit alone do not imply that these limits differ: the hit might carry arbitrarily little mass. Excluding that loss of information is the central step.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### From a cap hit to separation

Along a sequence of observation horizons shrinking to zero, rescale time and space at a cap time $t_{*,N}$ by $r_N=t_{*,N}+s_N$, and divide the hot density by $r_N$. The resulting cap is of order one. The cold density has a different normalization: multiplying it by $r_N$ produces a locally bounded velocity measure at positive times. Its two axial velocity signs coexist in the limit because the increasingly fine spatial stripes average to their occupied fractions. Strong convergence of hot velocity averages identifies the cold–hot product. This use of velocity averaging follows the uniformly integrable formulation of (Golse et al. 1988, Proposition 3) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[GolseLionsPerthameSentis1988](#ref-GolseLionsPerthameSentis1988)]. The limit obeys a linear Boltzmann equation in that two-sign background. This dilation is distinct from the fixed-horizon compactness used to construct admissible solutions.

To show that the dilated hot limit is nonzero, we analyze normalized Carleman gain kernels. Carleman’s representation expresses a collision gain as an integral over velocity planes; its normalization here is derived in Section <a href="#ker:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ker:section">3</a>. Iterated-gain regularization has an earlier role in the homogeneous theory (Mischler and Wennberg 1999, Lemma 2.1) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[MischlerWennberg1999](#ref-MischlerWennberg1999)]. In the present construction the estimates must be uniform as the cold velocity distributions collapse onto the axis, despite the singularity of $H$. Two complementary arguments provide this control. Near the velocity axis, two successive gains have vanishing normalized mass: the first has a uniformly non-atomic axial input, while a second large gain would force that input to match a small collection of characteristic cold speeds. Away from the axis, five gains suffice. Their times and angular parameters map to final time, position, and velocity with rank seven almost everywhere. A compact submersion argument then turns vanishing local hot mass into a vanishing gain contribution. Together these facts prevent the cap hit from disappearing in the weak limit.

The limiting hot equation also has a branching interpretation. A hot particle colliding with a cold jet creates two hot outputs. We retain finite collision histories with bounded velocities and show that their mass grows by a fixed factor inside an expanding spatial box. If the dilated observation horizon were infinite, this growth would exceed the polynomial mass bound supplied by the cap. The horizon is therefore finite, so the cap scale remains comparable to the physical observation horizon along the extracted sequence.

Finally, the dormant solution vanishes off the velocity axis under the corresponding shrinking-horizon dilation. If cap limits agreed with it at arbitrarily small fixed horizons, a diagonal choice of approximants would force the nonzero dilated hot limit to vanish as well. This contradiction yields two distinct early solutions of the original equation with the same datum. The periodic existence theorem then continues each solution globally while preserving admissibility. Figure <a href="#fig:proof-route" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:proof-route">1</a> separates the two compactness procedures and their roles in this final comparison.

<figure id="fig:proof-route">

<figcaption>Proof structure. At each fixed physical horizon, the two approximation families yield admissible solutions with the same initial datum. Separately, cap approximants on shrinking horizons are dilated about a cap hit. Nonconcentration preserves a nonzero hot limit, and branching prevents its scale from vanishing relative to the horizon along the extracted sequence. These facts contradict equality with the dormant limit, whose corresponding dilation vanishes off the velocity axis.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Organization

Section <a href="#sec:framework" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:framework">2</a> gives the full admissibility definition and the colored equations. Section <a href="#ker:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ker:section">3</a> establishes uniform collision-kernel estimates, and Section <a href="#jet:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="jet:section">4</a> constructs the datum and controls its cold components. Section <a href="#sec:cap" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:cap">5</a> selects the approximation families; Section <a href="#sec:admissibility" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:admissibility">6</a> constructs their fixed-horizon limits and verifies admissibility. Section <a href="#sec:dilation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:dilation">7</a> identifies the dilated linear equation, and Section <a href="#nc:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="nc:section">8</a> proves that its hot limit is nonzero. Section <a href="#sec:branching" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:branching">9</a> excludes an infinite dilated horizon. Section <a href="#sec:separation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:separation">10</a> completes the same-datum comparison and global continuation.

<a id="sec:framework"></a>
### Equation, admissibility, and notation

The spatial torus $\mathbb T^3=\mathbb R^3/\mathbb Z^3$ has volume one, and $\Omega=\mathbb T^3_x\times\mathbb R^3_v$. We write $x=(y,z)$, with two transverse coordinates $y$ and one axial coordinate $z$, and $v=(\bar v,v_3)$. In a periodic lift these coordinates range in $\mathbb R^2\times\mathbb R$. All spatially rescaled limits below are taken in the lift. We write $D_t=\partial_t+v\cdot\nabla_x$ for free transport.

<a id="eq:collision"></a>
For incoming velocities $v,v_*$ and $\omega\in S^2$, set $$a=(v-v_*)\cdot\omega,\qquad
v'=v-a\omega,\qquad v_*'=v_*+a\omega.$$ The surface measure on $S^2$ is its ordinary area measure. We use $$\label{eq:collision}
\begin{split}
Q(F,F)&=Q^+(F,F)-Q^-(F,F),\\
Q^+(F,F)(v)&=\int_{\mathbb R^3\times S^2}|a|F(v')F(v_*')\,\mathrm dv_*\,\mathrm d\omega,\\
Q^-(F,F)(v)&=F(v)\nu_F(v).
\end{split}$$ Arguments $t,x$, identical in every factor, are suppressed. The associated symmetric gain is denoted $P(f,g)$, so that $P(F,F)=Q^+(F,F)$. Its symmetry in the hard-sphere normalization is included in Lemma <a href="#lem:carleman" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:carleman">3</a>. The loss frequency is <a id="eq:frequency"></a>$$\label{eq:frequency}
\nu_f(v)=2\pi\int_{\mathbb R^3}|v-w|f(w)\,\mathrm dw.$$ A superscript $b$ on $P,\nu,Q$ means that the same factor $\mathbf 1_{\{|v-v_*|<b\}}$ is inserted into gain and loss. This cutoff is invariant under elastic collision.

Put $$M(v)=(2\pi)^{-3/2}e^{-|v|^2/2},\qquad G=F/M,$$ and define $$\mathcal H_M(F)=\int_\Omega
 \bigl(F\log(F/M)-F+M\bigr)\,\mathrm dx\,\mathrm dv.$$ The entropy dissipation is $$\mathcal D(F)=\frac14
\int_{\mathbb T^3\times\mathbb R^3\times\mathbb R^3\times S^2}
|a|\,(F'F_*'-FF_*)\log\frac{F'F_*'}{FF_*}
 \,\mathrm dx\,\mathrm dv\,\mathrm dv_*\,\mathrm d\omega.$$ The nonnegative integrand is assigned its lower-semicontinuous extended value when a factor vanishes. We use $0|\log0|=0$.

<a id="stmt-def-admissible"></a>
<div id="def:admissible" class="definition">

**Definition 2** (Entropy-class solution). For a nonnegative datum $F_0$ satisfying $$\int_\Omega F_0(1+|v|^2+|\log F_0|)\,\mathrm dx\,\mathrm dv<\infty,$$ an admissible global solution is a nonnegative $F$ with the following properties.

1.  $F\in C_w([0,\infty);L^1(\Omega))$, with $F(0)=F_0$, where weak continuity means continuity against every bounded measurable test. On each bounded time interval, $\int_\Omega F(1+|v|^2+|\log F|)$ is uniformly bounded.

2.  $Q^\pm(F,F)/(1+G)$ belong to $L^1([0,T]\times\Omega)$ for every finite $T$. For every $\beta\in C^1([0,\infty))$ with $\beta(0)=0$ and $|\beta'(z)|\le C_\beta/(1+z)$, and every smooth test $\varphi(t,x,v)$ periodic in $x$ and compactly supported in $[0,\infty)\times\mathbb R^3_v$, $$\begin{split}
    &\int_0^\infty\!\!\int_\Omega
     \bigl[M\beta(G)(\partial_t\varphi+v\cdot\nabla_x\varphi)
          +\beta'(G)Q(F,F)\varphi\bigr]\,\mathrm dx\,\mathrm dv\,\mathrm dt\\
    &\hspace{2cm}
    +\int_\Omega M\beta(F_0/M)\varphi(0,x,v)\,\mathrm dx\,\mathrm dv=0 .
    \end{split}$$

3.  With $\rho=\int F\,\mathrm dv$ and $j=\int vF\,\mathrm dv$, $\partial_t\rho+\nabla_x\cdot j=0$ distributionally with the initial density $\int F_0\,\mathrm dv$. Total momentum is conserved. For every $t\ge0$, $$\int_\Omega |v|^2F(t)\le\int_\Omega |v|^2F_0,\qquad
    \mathcal H_M(F(t))+\int_0^t\mathcal D(F(s))\,\mathrm ds
     \le\mathcal H_M(F_0).$$

</div>

The same definition on a finite interval uses tests and traces on that interval. We use the global existence theory (DiPerna and Lions 1989, 1991; Levermore and Masmoudi 2010) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[DiPernaLions1989](#ref-DiPernaLions1989), [DiPernaLions1991](#ref-DiPernaLions1991), [LevermoreMasmoudi2010](#ref-LevermoreMasmoudi2010)] only to continue solutions already constructed on a short interval. In particular, we do not require local momentum or local energy conservation as additional admissibility conditions.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### A velocity weight and colored components

<a id="eq:weight"></a>
Fix $1<q<2$ and $m=100$. The weight used throughout is $$\label{eq:weight}
H(v)=e^{-|v|^2}\langle v\rangle^{-m}
       \bigl(1+|\bar v|^{-q}\bigr),\qquad
\langle v\rangle=(1+|v|^2)^{1/2}.$$ Its values on the velocity axis can be assigned arbitrarily; all weighted supremum estimates are essential estimates. It is integrable with every polynomial velocity weight and lies in $L^p$ for some $p>1$. Write $\|f\|_H=\mathop{\mathrm{ess\,sup}}_{x,v}|f(x,v)|/H(v)$.

<a id="eq:colored"></a>
The cold components are $c_i$, their sum is $c$, and $h$ denotes the hot component. Particles retain a cold label only in collisions within that label. All other collisions create hot particles. With $F=c+h$, this gives $$\label{eq:colored}
\begin{split}
(D_t)c_i&=P(c_i,c_i)-c_i\nu_F,\\
(D_t)h&=2P(c,h)+P(h,h)+\sum_{i\ne j}P(c_i,c_j)-h\nu_F.
\end{split}$$ The sum over $i\ne j$ is ordered. Summing these equations gives $(D_t)F=Q(F,F)$. This identity also holds with a common collision cutoff. When labels include periodic copies, the individual functions are defined in the lift and are covariant under translations; their sums are periodic.

Constants denoted $C$, with or without subscripts, may depend on the fixed construction parameters. They never depend on the approximation index or the shrinking time horizon unless explicitly stated. The parameters needed for branching growth will be fixed before the final small geometric and time parameters. Section <a href="#sec:branching" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:branching">9</a> records the order of choices.

<a id="ker:section"></a>
### Collision kernels and a velocity majorant

The construction in the next section uses cold velocity distributions with arbitrarily small transverse widths. We seek bounds for $P(c,H)/H$ that remain uniform as those widths tend to zero, together with a bound for $P(H,H)/H$. The later nonconcentration argument needs more than bounded total gain: it also needs control of the complete normalized measure in the cold and hot input velocities. We obtain both kinds of estimates below.

Write $v=(\bar v,v_3)\in\mathbb R^2\times\mathbb R$ and set $$W(v)=1+|\bar v|^{-q},\qquad
 H(v)=e^{-|v|^2}\langle v\rangle^{-m}W(v),
 \qquad 1<q<2,\quad m=100,$$ as in <a href="#eq:weight" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:weight">[eq:weight]</a>. Values on the velocity axis are irrelevant to all the almost-everywhere estimates below. The function $H$ has every polynomial moment and belongs to $L^p(\mathbb R^3)$ whenever $1\leq p<2/q$. Indeed, its only local singularity is $|\bar v|^{-q}$ in two dimensions, and its decay at infinity is Gaussian.

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### The exact Carleman formula

The hyperplane representation used here is a Carleman formula; see also (Gamba, Panferov, and Villani 2009, Appendix C) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[GambaPanferovVillani2009](#ref-GambaPanferovVillani2009)]. We derive its coefficient for the normalization of this paper.

<a id="stmt-lem-carleman"></a>
<div id="lem:carleman" class="lemma">

<a id="ker:carleman"></a>
**Lemma 3** (Collision geometry). *For the normalization in <a href="#eq:collision" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:collision">[eq:collision]</a>, the symmetric bilinear gain is $$\label{ker:carleman}
 P(c,h)(v)
 =2\int_{\mathbb R^3}c(p)\,\,\mathrm dp
       \int_{\mathbb R^3}
       \delta\bigl((v-p)\cdot(\xi-v)\bigr)h(\xi)\,\,\mathrm d\xi.$$ For fixed $p\ne v$, the inner delta denotes surface measure on the plane $(v-p)\cdot(\xi-v)=0$, divided by $|v-p|$. For incoming velocities $p,\xi$, the outgoing measure has total mass $2\pi|p-\xi|$ and is uniform on the sphere with diameter $[p,\xi]$. Formula <a href="#ker:carleman" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ker:carleman">[ker:carleman]</a> also defines the gain when $c$ is a nonnegative velocity measure and $h$ is a nonnegative density, whenever the displayed integral is finite almost everywhere.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-carleman"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-carleman</summary>

<a id="ker:sphere-law"></a>
*Proof.* Put $g=p-\xi$. Reflection sends $g$ to $g-2(g\cdot\omega)\omega$. On either hemisphere of the normal sphere, the change from the polar angle of $\omega$ to the reflected direction shows that $|g\cdot\omega|\,\,\mathrm d\omega$ pushes forward to $(|g|/4)\,\,\mathrm d\sigma$. The two hemispheres therefore give $(|g|/2)\,\,\mathrm d\sigma$, where $\sigma\in S^2$ is the outgoing relative direction. Equivalently, for every nonnegative test function $\psi$, the one-output measure is $$\label{ker:sphere-law}
 \frac{|p-\xi|}{2}\int_{S^2}
 \psi\left(\frac{p+\xi}{2}
                 +\frac{|p-\xi|}{2}\sigma\right)\,\mathrm d\sigma.$$ Its mass is $2\pi|p-\xi|$. The affine map in this integral multiplies surface area by $|p-\xi|^2/4$, so the density on its image sphere is $2/|p-\xi|$.

The same sphere is the zero set of $f(v)=(v-p)\cdot(\xi-v)$. On that sphere, $|\nabla_v f(v)|=|p-\xi|$. The coarea formula consequently identifies the measure in <a href="#ker:sphere-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ker:sphere-law">[ker:sphere-law]</a> with $2\delta(f(v))\,\,\mathrm dv$. Integration against the two incoming densities and Tonelli’s theorem give <a href="#ker:carleman" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ker:carleman">[ker:carleman]</a>. The usual pre/post-collisional change of variables identifies this incoming-pair expression with the gain in <a href="#eq:collision" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:collision">[eq:collision]</a>. Coincident incoming velocities have zero collision rate. The same identities can first be integrated against a velocity measure $c(\,\mathrm dp)$, proving the asserted extension. ◻

</details>

The cutoff gain obtained by inserting $\mathbf 1_{|p-\xi|<b}$ is bounded by the uncut gain. Thus all upper estimates in this section also hold for the collision cutoffs used later.

<a id="pm-h-11"></a>
#### A geometric slice estimate

For $d>0$, let $\mathcal G_d$ be the centered transverse Gaussian probability measure with density $$\frac{1}{\pi d^2}e^{-|\bar p|^2/d^2},$$ and let $\mathcal G_0=\delta_0$. We shall also use the uniform probability measure $\mathcal U_d$ on $\{\bar p:|\bar p|<d\}$.

<a id="stmt-ker-geometric-slice"></a>
<div id="ker:geometric-slice" class="lemma">

<a id="ker:geometric-total"></a>
<a id="ker:geometric-interval"></a>
**Lemma 4** (Transverse averaging and axial slices). *Let $\lambda$ be a probability mixture of the measures $\mathcal U_d$, $d>0$, and $\delta_0$, and let $\rho$ be any axial probability measure. No bound on its density or its support is required. Put $c=\lambda\otimes\rho$. For $R\geq1$, $\bar v\ne0$, and an interval $I\subset\mathbb R$ of length $0<\ell\leq2R$, one has $$\begin{aligned}
 &\frac{1}{W(v)}\int c(\,\mathrm dp)
   \int_{|\xi-v|\leq R}
    \delta\bigl((v-p)\cdot(\xi-v)\bigr)W(\xi)\,\,\mathrm d\xi
     \leq C_q R^2,\label{ker:geometric-total}\\
 &\frac{1}{W(v)}\int c(\,\mathrm dp)
   \int_{\substack{|\xi-v|\leq R\\ \xi_3\in I}}
    \delta\bigl((v-p)\cdot(\xi-v)\bigr)W(\xi)\,\,\mathrm d\xi
     \leq C_q\bigl(R\ell+R^{q-1}\ell^{2-q}\bigr).
     \label{ker:geometric-interval}
\end{aligned}$$ The constants are independent of all transverse widths and of $\rho$. In particular, these statements hold with $\lambda=\mathcal G_d$ for every $d\geq0$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-ker-geometric-slice"><summary>Proof of stmt-ker-geometric-slice</summary>

<a id="ker:line-integral"></a>
*Proof.* Set $r=|\bar v|$, $n=\bar v-\bar p$, and $e_n=n/|n|$. Except on a set of transverse measure zero, $n\ne0$. For fixed $p_3,\xi_3$, the delta in the transverse variables is line measure divided by $|n|$, on a line whose signed distance from zero is $$A-B(\xi_3-v_3),\qquad
 A=e_n\cdot\bar v,\quad B=\frac{v_3-p_3}{|n|}.$$ The regular part of its integral over $|\xi-v|\leq R$ is at most $2R/|n|$. The singular part is bounded by its integral over the full line, namely $$\label{ker:line-integral}
 \frac{1}{|n|}\int_{\mathbb R}
      \bigl((A-B(\xi_3-v_3))^2+s^2\bigr)^{-q/2}\,\,\mathrm ds
 =\frac{a_q}{|n|}|A-B(\xi_3-v_3)|^{1-q},$$ where $a_q=\int_{\mathbb R}(1+s^2)^{-q/2}\,\,\mathrm ds<\infty$ because $q>1$. Infinite values on a zero-distance slice are harmless in the nonnegative iterated integral.

<a id="ker:scalar-interval"></a>
For every interval $J\subset[-R,R]$ of length at most $\ell$, $$\label{ker:scalar-interval}
 \int_J |A-Bx|^{1-q}\,\,\mathrm dx
 \leq C_q R^{q-1}\ell^{2-q}|A|^{1-q}.$$ If $2R|B|\leq|A|$, the integrand is at most $C_q|A|^{1-q}$, and the assertion follows from $\ell\leq2R$. Otherwise, integration about the possible root gives an upper bound $C_q|B|^{1-q}\ell^{2-q}$; since $1-q<0$ and $|B|>|A|/(2R)$, this proves <a href="#ker:scalar-interval" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ker:scalar-interval">[ker:scalar-interval]</a>. When $A=0$ the displayed upper bound is understood in the extended sense.

<a id="ker:transverse-expectation"></a>
We next prove $$\label{ker:transverse-expectation}
 \int\frac{1+|e_n\cdot e_{\bar v}|^{1-q}}{|n|}\,
          \lambda(\,\mathrm d\bar p)\leq\frac{C_q}{r}.$$ It suffices to consider a disk of radius $d$. If $d\leq r/2$, then $|n|\geq r/2$ and $e_n\cdot e_{\bar v}\geq1/3$, which proves the bound. If $d>r/2$, use polar coordinates for $n$. Its range lies in $|n|<r+d<3d$. The factor $|n|^{-1}$ cancels the radial Jacobian, while $$\int_0^{2\pi}\bigl(1+|\cos\theta|^{1-q}\bigr)\,\,\mathrm d\theta<\infty$$ because $q<2$. Division by the disk area gives $C_q/d\leq2C_q/r$. The point mass at zero satisfies the same estimate directly. Finally, the Gaussian is a probability mixture of these disks: for $d>0$ its mixture density in the disk radius $a$ is $$\frac{2a^3}{d^4}e^{-a^2/d^2}\,\,\mathrm da,$$ whose integral is one. This proves the Gaussian assertion as well.

Integrating <a href="#ker:line-integral" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ker:line-integral">[ker:line-integral]</a> in $\xi_3$, applying <a href="#ker:scalar-interval" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ker:scalar-interval">[ker:scalar-interval]</a>, and then <a href="#ker:transverse-expectation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ker:transverse-expectation">[ker:transverse-expectation]</a>, bounds the expression in <a href="#ker:geometric-interval" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ker:geometric-interval">[ker:geometric-interval]</a> by $$C_q R\ell\frac{r^{-1}}{1+r^{-q}}
 +C_qR^{q-1}\ell^{2-q}\frac{r^{-q}}{1+r^{-q}}.$$ Both fractions are bounded for $q>1$. These bounds are independent of $p_3$, so integration against the arbitrary probability $\rho$ preserves them. Taking the entire axial range of length $2R$ also gives <a href="#ker:geometric-total" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ker:geometric-total">[ker:geometric-total]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-12"></a>
#### Sheets and normalized kernel measures

<a id="stmt-ker-sheet-class"></a>
<div id="ker:sheet-class" class="definition">

**Definition 5** (Uniform sheet class). Fix $L\geq1$. An elementary sheet in $\mathcal S_L$ is a product probability measure <a id="ker:sheet-definition"></a>$$\label{ker:sheet-definition}
 c(\,\mathrm dp)=\mathcal G_d(\,\mathrm d\bar p)\rho(\,\mathrm dp_3),\qquad
 0\leq d\leq\tfrac18,\qquad
 \int_{\mathbb R}e^{8a^2}\rho(\,\mathrm da)\leq L.$$ We also allow probability mixtures of elementary sheets. An upper bound by a sheet with amplitude $A_0$ means domination as measures by $A_0$ times such a mixture.

</div>

Every axial probability supported in $[-A,A]$ satisfies this definition with $L=e^{8A^2}$. In particular, axial atoms and limits of arbitrarily narrow axial densities are permitted. The exponential moment is used only to remove large axial velocities, and imposes no axial regularity.

<a id="ker:normalized-kernel"></a>
For such a sheet and $\bar v\ne0$, define its normalized kernel by $$\label{ker:normalized-kernel}
 \mathcal K_c(v;\,\mathrm dp\,\,\mathrm d\xi)
 =2\,c(\,\mathrm dp)\,
       \delta\bigl((v-p)\cdot(\xi-v)\bigr)
       \frac{H(\xi)}{H(v)}\,\,\mathrm d\xi.$$ The total mass is $P(c,H)(v)/H(v)$. Kernel restrictions always refer to this complete measure, including the ratio $H(\xi)/H(v)$.

<a id="stmt-lem-sheet-bound"></a>
<div id="lem:sheet-bound" class="lemma">

<a id="ker:sheet-gain"></a>
<a id="ker:shell-tail"></a>
**Lemma 6** (Uniform sheet gain bound). *For every $L<\infty$ there is $C_{q,m,L}<\infty$ such that $$\label{ker:sheet-gain}
 P(c,H)(v)\leq C_{q,m,L}H(v)
 \quad\hbox{for almost every }v,
 \qquad c\in\mathcal S_L.$$ More quantitatively, for $R\geq1$, $$\label{ker:shell-tail}
 \mathcal K_c\bigl(v;
      R<|p|+|\xi-v|\leq2R\bigr)
 \leq C_{q,m,L}R^2e^{-cR^2},$$ where $c>0$ is an absolute constant. The same conclusions, multiplied by $A_0$, hold under sheet domination with amplitude $A_0$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-sheet-bound"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-sheet-bound</summary>

<a id="ker:energy-factor"></a>
*Proof.* By positivity it suffices to treat an elementary sheet. Put $k=\xi-v$ and $w=p+k$. On the collision plane, $$\label{ker:energy-factor}
 |p|^2+|\xi|^2=|v|^2+|w|^2,
 \qquad
 \frac{H(\xi)}{H(v)}
 =e^{|p|^2-|w|^2}
    \frac{\langle v\rangle^m}{\langle\xi\rangle^m}
    \frac{W(\xi)}{W(v)}.$$ The same identity implies $\langle v\rangle\leq\langle p\rangle\langle\xi\rangle$. Since $\langle p\rangle^m\leq C_m e^{|p|^2}$, the kernel is bounded by <a id="ker:tilted-kernel"></a>$$\label{ker:tilted-kernel}
 C_m e^{2|p|^2}c(\,\mathrm dp)\,
 e^{-|w|^2}\delta\bigl((v-p)\cdot k\bigr)
       \frac{W(v+k)}{W(v)}\,\,\mathrm dk.$$

<a id="ker:transverse-tilt"></a>
<a id="ker:axial-tilt-tail"></a>
The tilted cold measure retains a product majorant. For $0<d\leq1/8$, $$\label{ker:transverse-tilt}
 e^{2|\bar p|^2}\mathcal G_d(\,\mathrm d\bar p)
 \leq4e^{-46|\bar p|^2}\mathcal G_{2d}(\,\mathrm d\bar p).$$ This follows by dividing the two Gaussian densities: their ratio is $4\exp[-(3/(4d^2)-2)|\bar p|^2]$, and $3/(4d^2)-2\geq46$. The assertion at $d=0$ has the evident interpretation. The axial tilted measure $\widetilde\rho(\,\mathrm da)=e^{2a^2}\rho(\,\mathrm da)$ has mass at most $L$, and satisfies $$\label{ker:axial-tilt-tail}
 \widetilde\rho\{|a|>u\}\leq Le^{-6u^2},\qquad u\geq0.$$ Consequently $e^{2|p|^2}c(\,\mathrm dp)$ is bounded by $4L$ times a product of a centered transverse Gaussian and an axial probability. Moreover, its restriction to $|p|>u$ is bounded by $CLe^{-cu^2}$ times a sum of at most two such product probabilities. To see the last assertion, split into $|\bar p|>u/\sqrt2$ and $|p_3|>u/\sqrt2$, and apply <a href="#ker:transverse-tilt" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ker:transverse-tilt">[ker:transverse-tilt]</a> and <a href="#ker:axial-tilt-tail" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ker:axial-tilt-tail">[ker:axial-tilt-tail]</a>, respectively. The axial probabilities obtained by normalization may have large support; Lemma <a href="#ker:geometric-slice" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ker:geometric-slice">4</a> permits this.

On the shell in <a href="#ker:shell-tail" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ker:shell-tail">[ker:shell-tail]</a>, either $|p|>R/4$ or $|w|>R/2$, because $|p|+|k|\leq2|p|+|w|$. In the first case the preceding tilted-measure tail contributes $CLe^{-cR^2}$. In the second case $e^{-|w|^2}\leq e^{-R^2/4}$. In either case the remaining integral has $|k|\leq2R$ and is bounded by $C_qR^2$ using Lemma <a href="#ker:geometric-slice" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ker:geometric-slice">4</a>.
