*Proof.* Given $v\in E$, choose a sign with $|v-\sigma e_3|\ge1$. Use this partner for the first collision, and select an output within a small fixed distance $\delta$ of $\sigma e_3$. Its probability has a uniform positive lower bound: the incident distance lies in $[1,6]$, and a cap of fixed Euclidean radius $\delta$ at that endpoint has area probability bounded below uniformly on the corresponding diameter spheres. Such first outputs lie strictly in $E$.

Use partner $-\sigma e_3$ next. If the first output is exactly $\sigma e_3$, the second sphere is the unit sphere; restrict its output $u$ near $e_1$. For the third collision use partner $e_3$. The last output is $$T(u,n)=\frac{u+e_3}{2}+\frac{|u-e_3|}{2}n.$$ At $u=e_1$, $n_0=-(e_1+e_3)/\sqrt2$, this output is zero. The two angular derivatives in $n$ span $(e_1+e_3)^\perp$. The vector $e_3$ is tangent to the preceding unit sphere at $e_1$, and direct differentiation gives <a id="br:rank-derivative"></a>$$\label{br:rank-derivative}
\partial_{e_3}T(e_1,n_0)=\frac{e_1+3e_3}{4},\qquad
(e_1+e_3)\cdot\partial_{e_3}T(e_1,n_0)=1.$$ Thus the map of the four angular coordinates in the last two collisions to the last velocity has rank three.

Take the two coordinates of $n$ and the coordinate of $u$ with derivative $e_3$ as distinguished variables, and hold the fourth coordinate in a short interval. The inverse function theorem, with this fourth coordinate and the first output as parameters, gives neighborhoods on which the distinguished-variable map is one-to-one, its Jacobian and inverse Jacobian are bounded, and its image contains a fixed ball $B(0,r_0)$. Shrink these neighborhoods and $\delta$ so this holds for every first output within $\delta$ of $\sigma e_3$, for both signs. Sphere area in these coordinates has a positive lower density. Change of variables in the three distinguished coordinates, and integration in the fourth, give a fixed positive multiple of volume on $B(0,r_0)$.

Force the three times into three fixed, disjoint intervals inside $(0,1)$, and prohibit other jumps. The first selected relative speed is at least 1, the second is close to 2, and the third close to $\sqrt2$. In the reference Markov process the selected transition intensity is $$4\pi|v-\sigma e_3|\,
\mathbf 1_E(\Phi(v,\sigma e_3,n))\,\mathrm d\varsigma(n).$$ It has positive uniform lower density on the patches just specified. The no-additional-jump factor is at least $e^{-C_0}$. Integrating the times proves <a href="#br:doeblin" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="br:doeblin">[br:doeblin]</a>. Keeping only the first two collisions, selecting a small patch near $e_1$ in the second, and prohibiting later jumps proves the last assertion. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-br-mixing"></a>
<div id="br:mixing" class="lemma">

**Lemma 34**. *The reference process has a stationary probability law $\pi$ with $\int v\,\mathrm d\pi(v)=0$. There are $C,c>0$ such that $$\sup_{v\in E}\|P_t(v,\cdot)-\pi\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\le Ce^{-ct},
\qquad
\sup_{v\in E}\mathbb E_v\left|
A^{-1}\int_0^A V_s\,\mathrm ds\right|^2\le\frac C A\quad(A\ge1).$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-br-mixing"><summary>Proof of stmt-br-mixing</summary>

*Proof.* The normalized measure on the right side of <a href="#br:doeblin" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="br:doeblin">[br:doeblin]</a> is a common part of positive mass $\epsilon$ in all rows of $P_1$. Removing this common part shows that the map $\mu\mapsto\mu P_1$ contracts total variation distance by $1-\epsilon$. Probability measures form a complete space in that metric, so iteration yields a unique fixed point $\pi$ and uniform geometric convergence. Since $P_t$ commutes with $P_1$, $\pi P_t$ is another fixed point of $P_1$, hence equals $\pi$. Contractivity of Markov kernels gives the estimate between integer times.

Negation of velocity interchanges the two signs and preserves $E$, so uniqueness of $\pi$ makes it symmetric and its mean zero. Consequently $\sup_{v\in E}|\mathbb E_vV_t|\le Ce^{-ct}$. The Markov property and bounded velocities imply $$|\mathbb E(V_s\cdot V_t)|\le Ce^{-c(t-s)}\quad(0\le s\le t)$$ uniformly over starting states. Integrating this estimate on $[0,A]^2$ bounds the second moment of $\int_0^A V_s\,\mathrm ds$ by $CA$. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-45"></a>
#### A displacement block and measurable perturbations

<a id="br:kappa-choice"></a>
Fix $d=1/10$, and choose once and for all $$\label{br:kappa-choice}
0<\kappa<\min\left\{\frac14,\frac{c_0}{2C_0},\frac{r_0}{4}\right\}.$$ In particular a small ball about $\kappa e_1$ lies in the minorization ball.

To repeat growth in the cold background, we will transport mass through boxes whose transverse distance from the axis grows proportionally to time: $$\mathcal B_t=\{(x,v):|x-\kappa te_1|\le d\kappa t,\ |v|\le D\},
\qquad t>0.$$ For a duration $\Delta>0$, a displacement within $d\kappa\Delta$ of $\kappa\Delta e_1$ carries every starting point of $\mathcal B_t$ into $\mathcal B_{t+\Delta}$, provided the final velocity remains in $E$. We first obtain growth with this displacement in the reference medium, then transfer it to the varying cold background.

<a id="stmt-br-reference-block"></a>
<div id="br:reference-block" class="lemma">

**Lemma 35**. *There is a finite $L$ such that, for all $$A\in I_L:=\left[\frac L{1+d},\frac L{1-d}\right],\qquad v\in E,$$ reference branching histories with velocities in $E$, final velocity strictly inside $E$, and displacement $Z_A$ satisfying <a id="br:strict-drift"></a>$$\label{br:strict-drift}
|Z_A-\kappa Ae_1|<\frac34d\kappa A$$ have total weight greater than 8.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-br-reference-block"><summary>Proof of stmt-br-reference-block</summary>

*Proof.* Put $A_0=(1-\kappa)A-1$. On the first interval of length $A_0$, require the reference Markov flight to have integrated velocity of norm at most $Ad\kappa/4$. Lemma <a href="#br:mixing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="br:mixing">34</a> and Chebyshev’s inequality give probability at least $1/2$, uniformly in the start when $A$ is sufficiently large. Over the next unit interval require the last velocity to lie in $B(e_1,d/4)$. The conditional probability is at least a fixed $\varepsilon>0$, by Lemma <a href="#br:minorization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="br:minorization">33</a>. Require no jump during the final interval of length $\kappa A$, whose conditional probability is at least $e^{-C_0\kappa A}$.

The errors in displacement from $\kappa Ae_1$ on the three intervals are at most $Ad\kappa/4$, $D$, and $Ad\kappa/4$. For $A>4D/(d\kappa)$ their sum is less than $3Ad\kappa/4$. The final velocity lies strictly in $E$. Lemma <a href="#br:retention" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="br:retention">32</a> bounds the branching weight below by $$\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\exp((c_0-C_0\kappa)A).$$ Its exponent is positive by <a href="#br:kappa-choice" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="br:kappa-choice">[br:kappa-choice]</a>. Choose $L$ large enough that this exceeds 8, and all the preceding lower bounds on $A$ hold throughout $I_L$. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-br-robust-block"></a>
<div id="br:robust-block" class="lemma">

<a id="br:medium-closeness"></a>
**Lemma 36**. *For these fixed $\kappa,L$, there are $J\in\mathbb N$ and $\varepsilon_*\in(0,1/4)$ with the following property. Suppose a measurable medium obeys, throughout every speed-$D$ excursion from a specified starting point over a duration $A\in I_L$, $$\label{br:medium-closeness}
|b_\sigma-1|\le\varepsilon_*,
\qquad |w_\sigma-\sigma e_3|\le\varepsilon_*
\quad(\sigma=\pm1).$$ From each starting velocity in $E$, histories with at most $J$ collisions, speed at most $D$ on every free flight, every postcollision velocity and the final velocity strictly inside $E$, and <a id="br:robust-drift"></a>$$\label{br:robust-drift}
|Z_A-\kappa Ae_1|<d\kappa A$$ have total weight greater than 3. The initial free flight may have speed $D$. No continuity of the medium is needed.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-br-robust-block"><summary>Proof of stmt-br-robust-block</summary>

*Proof.* Fix a reference start and duration $(v,A)\in E\times I_L$. The zero-collision term has weight $e^{-\nu_0(v)A}\le1$. Discarding it from the family in Lemma <a href="#br:reference-block" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="br:reference-block">35</a> leaves weight greater than 7. Monotone convergence in the positive history count permits restricting to finitely many counts $j\ge1$ while retaining weight greater than 7. Every postcollision velocity lies strictly in $E$ almost surely: the intersection of a nondegenerate diameter sphere with $\partial E$ has sphere area zero, and a degenerate collision has zero rate. This includes the final velocity. No inward margin is required for the initial velocity. The displacement inequality is strict. Ordered times may be kept apart and away from the endpoints. Using finitely many sphere coordinate charts and inner regularity of their parameter integrals, restrict further to compact parameter sets with total weight greater than 6 and positive margins in all these restrictions, including $|Z_A-\kappa Ae_1|<d\kappa A$. Parameterize the times as fractions of $A$.

The reference paths and weights are continuous in these finite parameters and in $(v,A)$. The fixed compact tests therefore retain weight greater than 5 and at least half their original positive margins in a sufficiently small relative neighborhood of that start and duration in $E\times I_L$. These neighborhoods cover the compact set $E\times I_L$. A finite subcover gives finitely many tests, a maximum count $J$, and uniform positive margins.

Use the same initial velocity, signs, directions, and fractional times for each perturbed history and its reference history. Their first flights have speed at most $D$, including boundary starts. The sphere map satisfies $$|\Phi(v,w,n)-\Phi(\widetilde v,\widetilde w,n)|
\le |v-\widetilde v|+|w-\widetilde w|.$$ Induction bounds the velocity error after $j$ collisions by $j\varepsilon_*$ and the displacement error by $AJ\varepsilon_*$. This comparison does not compare the medium at two nearby positions: its value at the actual perturbed position is close to the same constant reference partner. A sufficiently small $\varepsilon_*$ preserves all the strict velocity and displacement margins. The actual paths thus have speed at most $D$, so they stay within the region on which closeness was assumed. Formally this follows by induction up to the first possible failure; the preserved margins exclude such a failure.

The relative-speed errors and full loss-rate errors on these histories are bounded by $C_J\varepsilon_*$. The density error gives the same bound for the gain-rate factors. The interval has bounded length, so the error in the integrated loss and its exponential is bounded by $C_{J,L}\varepsilon_*$. Finite products of the bounded gain factors then show that <a href="#br:history-weight" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="br:history-weight">[br:history-weight]</a> changes by at most $C_{J,L}\varepsilon_*$ uniformly on each test. The tests have finite parameter measure. Decreasing $\varepsilon_*$ makes their integrals greater than 3. All coefficient estimates were pointwise; measurability is sufficient. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-46"></a>
#### Uniform choices of the construction parameters

<a id="br:access-velocity"></a>
We first reserve one more small geometric tolerance. There is $\alpha_{\mathrm{acc}}>0$, depending only on $\kappa$ and Lemma <a href="#br:minorization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="br:minorization">33</a>, with this property: after a first collision puts a velocity sufficiently close to $\sigma e_3$, two more collisions, with partners within $\alpha_{\mathrm{acc}}$ of $-\sigma e_3,e_3$, have positive angular probability of ending in $$\label{br:access-velocity}
B(\kappa e_1,d\kappa/4).$$ Indeed the inverse-coordinate construction in that lemma, with first output exactly $\sigma e_3$, maps a positive-measure angular set into $B(\kappa e_1,d\kappa/8)$, which is inside $B(0,r_0)$. Keep a compact subset of positive measure in this set. Its outputs have a positive distance from the complement of <a href="#br:access-velocity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="br:access-velocity">[br:access-velocity]</a>; the intermediate velocities and relative speeds remain in the neighborhoods of that construction. Uniform continuity of the two sphere maps preserves this for sufficiently small perturbations of the first output and partners, including measurable partners at the actual path positions. Take the smaller tolerance for the two possible signs. We also require $\alpha_{\mathrm{acc}}$ to be less than half the permitted radius of the first-output neighborhood.

<a id="br:moving-box"></a>
<a id="br:comparison-quantities"></a>
The constants $\kappa,L,J,\varepsilon_*,\alpha_{\mathrm{acc}}$ are now fixed and do not involve any construction parameter. Fix preliminary bounds $\eta\le1$, $\alpha\le1$, $K\ge1$. For $t>0$, put $$\label{br:moving-box}
\Delta=\frac{L\kappa t}{\theta K},
\qquad a=\frac{L\kappa}{\theta K}.$$ At a starting point $x_0=(y_0,z_0)$ in $\mathcal B_t$, $$(1-d)\kappa t\le|y_0|\le(1+d)\kappa t.$$ Every speed-$D$ excursion over $[t,t+\Delta]$ satisfies $$\big||y|-|y_0|\big|\le D\Delta,\qquad
|z|\le d\kappa t+D\Delta.$$ Set $\delta_K=DL/(\theta K(1-d))$. By increasing $K$, impose $\delta_K\le1/4$ and $a\le1/4$. Then $$|y|\ge(1-\delta_K)(1-d)\kappa t.$$ For all sufficiently large $t$, every such excursion is outside the lower mesh hole, since $l\le1$. On its active annulus $e^{-\eta}|y|\le\mu\le|y|$, for every mesh phase. Thus, with a fixed constant $C_1$ depending only on $d,D$, $$\label{br:comparison-quantities}
\begin{split}
\left|\frac{|y_0|}{\mu}-1\right|&\le C_1(\eta+\delta_K),\\
\frac{\tau}{\mu}&\le\frac{C_1}{\kappa},\\
\frac{|z-\tau u_\sigma|}{\mu}&\le\frac{C_1}{\kappa}
\qquad(t\le\tau\le t+\Delta).
\end{split}$$ The last estimate uses $|u_\sigma|\le2$.

<a id="br:duration-scaling"></a>
<a id="br:uniform-medium-error"></a>
Use the reference density factor $\rho_0=\theta K/|y_0|$, and multiply both time and position units by $\rho_0$; velocities are unchanged. The reference duration is exactly $$\label{br:duration-scaling}
A=\rho_0\Delta=\frac{L\kappa t}{|y_0|}\in I_L.$$ With $S_\sigma=S(\gamma(z-\tau u_\sigma)/\mu)$, the density ratio in these units is <a id="br:density-ratio"></a>$$\label{br:density-ratio}
\frac{b_\sigma(\tau,x)}{\rho_0}
=\frac{|y_0|}{\mu}\,
\frac{S_\sigma}{1+(\tau\alpha\gamma/\mu)S_\sigma}.$$ Because $1-\operatorname{sech}^2 h=\tanh^2h\le h^2$, <a href="#br:comparison-quantities" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="br:comparison-quantities">[br:comparison-quantities]</a> gives, when the right-hand small quantities are at most one, $$\label{br:uniform-medium-error}
\left|\frac{b_\sigma}{\rho_0}-1\right|
\le C_2\left(\eta+\delta_K+
              (\gamma/\kappa)^2+\alpha\gamma/\kappa\right),
\qquad |u_\sigma-\sigma|\le\alpha.$$ Here $C_2$ is independent of $t,l$, and mesh phase.

<a id="br:growth-exponent"></a>
Choose $\eta>0$ small, then $$0<\alpha<\min\{1/4,\varepsilon_*,\alpha_{\mathrm{acc}}\},$$ then $\gamma>0$ small, so that the first, third, and fourth terms on the right of <a href="#br:uniform-medium-error" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="br:uniform-medium-error">[br:uniform-medium-error]</a>, including their factor $C_2$, sum to less than $\varepsilon_*/2$. Finally increase $K$ so the $\delta_K$ term is less than $\varepsilon_*/2$, the preliminary restrictions hold, and $$\label{br:growth-exponent}
(1+a)^4<3.$$ This establishes <a href="#br:medium-closeness" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="br:medium-closeness">[br:medium-closeness]</a> for all the scaled blocks, with no choice depending on a limit, mesh, or solution.

These choices precede the final small geometric and thermal parameters. Making $\alpha\gamma$ small may increase the fixed coefficient in <a href="#br:global-rate" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="br:global-rate">[br:global-rate]</a> and earlier comparison bounds, but introduces no dependence on the approximation index. Propositions <a href="#prop:cold-control" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:cold-control">14</a> and <a href="#prop:finite-cap" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:finite-cap">16</a> allow $s_0,d_0$, and then the final short horizon $T_0$, to be chosen after these fixed constants. We use those choices henceforth.

<a id="pm-h-47"></a>
#### Access to a late box and the growth contradiction

<a id="stmt-br-accessibility"></a>
<div id="br:accessibility" class="lemma">

**Lemma 37**. *For the fixed parameters above, let $g\not\equiv0$ solve <a href="#eq:limit-equation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:limit-equation">[eq:limit-equation]</a> on $0<t<\infty$ with $0\le g\le(t+l)H$, for any allowed mesh and $l\in[0,1]$. Then $\int_{\mathcal B_t}g(t,x,v)\,\mathrm dx\,\mathrm dv>0$ at arbitrarily large finite times.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-br-accessibility"><summary>Proof of stmt-br-accessibility</summary>

*Proof.* At some positive time $a_0$, a bounded portion of $g(a_0)$ has positive mass. The velocity axis has Lebesgue measure zero, so a further portion of positive mass satisfies $$|x|\le R,\qquad |v|\le R,\qquad |\bar v|\ge\delta>0.$$ For a sufficiently long but finite free flight of length $s$, $$|y+s\bar v|\ge s\delta-R>l+2$$ on this portion. Its survival is bounded below by a positive constant by <a href="#br:global-rate" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="br:global-rate">[br:global-rate]</a>. Lemma <a href="#br:positive-histories" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="br:positive-histories">31</a> gives positive mass in a bounded region strictly outside the hole at time $a_1=a_0+s$.

Choose $h>0$ small so that paths whose initial speed is at most $R$ and whose later speeds are at most 3 remain in a bounded region with $|y|>l+1$ during $[a_1,a_1+h]$. Both sign densities have strictly positive lower bounds there. To see this directly, the time, axial coordinate, and transverse radius are bounded, the transverse radius has a positive lower bound, and $e^{-\eta}|y|\le\mu\le|y|$. The argument of $S$ is therefore bounded, its numerator is positive, and the denominator in <a href="#eq:background" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:background">[eq:background]</a> is bounded above. The rates on the selected bounded-speed paths also have finite upper bounds.

Confine three collisions to this short interval. Choose a reference sign with $|v-\sigma e_3|\ge1$ for the first collision. The actual relative speed is at least $1-\alpha\ge3/4$. Choose its output in a sufficiently small cap about the actual partner. The cap probability has a positive lower bound for inputs bounded by $R$. Since the actual partner is within $\alpha$ of $\sigma e_3$, this first output lies in the reserved first-output neighborhood. Next use the signs $-\sigma$ and $+1$, and the compact angular tests defining $\alpha_{\mathrm{acc}}$. Their final velocity lies in <a href="#br:access-velocity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="br:access-velocity">[br:access-velocity]</a>. The second output stays near $e_1$, so the second and third relative speeds have positive lower bounds; all postcollision speeds are at most 3. Put the three times in disjoint subintervals and prohibit other collisions. Positive lower bounds for the selected gain factors, angular probabilities, time-interval lengths, and survival give strictly positive total weight. The sphere-map comparison used here requires only uniform partner closeness, and therefore remains valid for measurable coefficients.

There is consequently a bounded positive portion at time $a_2=a_1+h$ with $|v-\kappa e_1|<d\kappa/4$. Let $R_2$ bound $|x-\kappa a_2e_1|$ on that portion. After free flight to any sufficiently large finite time $t$, $$|x+(t-a_2)v-\kappa te_1|
\le R_2+\frac{d\kappa}{4}(t-a_2)<d\kappa t.$$ Its speed is strictly less than $D$. For each finite $t$, survival remains strictly positive by <a href="#br:global-rate" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="br:global-rate">[br:global-rate]</a>. The zero-collision term of Lemma <a href="#br:positive-histories" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="br:positive-histories">31</a> proves the assertion. ◻

</details>

<details id="proof-stmt-br-accessibility-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-br-accessibility</summary>

*Proof of Proposition <a href="#prop:finite-dilated-horizon" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:finite-dilated-horizon">30</a>.* Suppose an extracted limit has $U=\infty$. Proposition <a href="#prop:nonzero-limit" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:nonzero-limit">21</a> makes it nonzero. Lemma <a href="#br:accessibility" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="br:accessibility">37</a> gives a sufficiently large $t_0$ with positive mass $m_0$ in $\mathcal B_{t_0}$. Increase $t_0$ if necessary so that all the excursions considered in <a href="#br:moving-box" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="br:moving-box">[br:moving-box]</a> lie above the lower hole. The same holds for every later block.

At each starting point of $\mathcal B_t$, apply Lemma <a href="#br:robust-block" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="br:robust-block">36</a> using the simultaneous space–time scaling <a href="#br:duration-scaling" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="br:duration-scaling">[br:duration-scaling]</a>. Translating back its displacement restriction gives $$|x_{\mathrm{end}}-x_{\mathrm{start}}-\kappa\Delta e_1|
<d\kappa\Delta.$$ The starting radius $d\kappa t$, followed by the triangle inequality, places the endpoint in $\mathcal B_{t+\Delta}$. The velocity belongs to $E$: the selected positive-count histories have a strictly interior final velocity. Their first flight need only have speed at most $D$, exactly as in the medium comparison. History weights are unchanged by this scaling: each gain rate is divided by $\rho_0$, each time differential is multiplied by $\rho_0$, and the integrated loss is invariant. The selected histories therefore have total weight greater than 3 uniformly over starts in $\mathcal B_t$.

Use the kernel obtained by integrating all histories with $1\le j\le J$ that satisfy the block’s velocity and displacement restrictions. The finite compact tests above certify its weight greater than 3; no choice of a test for each starting point is needed. Integrate this positive kernel against the portion of $g(t)$ in the box and use Lemma <a href="#br:positive-histories" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="br:positive-histories">31</a>. At $t_n=(1+a)^nt_0$, induction gives $$\int_{\mathcal B_{t_n}}g(t_n,x,v)\,\mathrm dx\,\mathrm dv\ge3^n m_0.$$ The cap and $l\le1$, on the other hand, give $$\int_{\mathcal B_{t_n}}g(t_n,x,v)\,\mathrm dx\,\mathrm dv
\le (t_n+1)\frac{4\pi}{3}(d\kappa t_n)^3\int_E H(v)\,\mathrm dv
\le C(1+t_n)^4.$$ This contradicts <a href="#br:growth-exponent" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="br:growth-exponent">[br:growth-exponent]</a> as $n\to\infty$. The positive mass $m_0$ and starting time may depend on the particular limit; their sizes are immaterial to this comparison. The parameters governing the blocks were fixed before the mesh and $l$ were chosen. Thus every extracted limit has finite dilated horizon. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:separation"></a>
### Separation of two solutions with the same datum

We now combine the two compactness procedures. All parameters have been fixed as in Proposition <a href="#prop:finite-dilated-horizon" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:finite-dilated-horizon">30</a>, with the final small parameters chosen to satisfy the preceding cold and cap estimates. Fix one dormant limit $F^{\mathrm d}$ on $[0,T_0]$, supplied by Proposition <a href="#prop:admissible-limits" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:admissible-limits">18</a>.

<a id="stmt-sep-dormant-vanishing"></a>
<div id="sep:dormant-vanishing" class="lemma">

**Lemma 38**. *As $T\downarrow0$, $$T^{-1}F^{\mathrm d}(Tt,Tx,v)\longrightarrow0$$ in distributions on compact subsets of $$0<t<1,\qquad x\in\mathbb R^3,\quad v\in\mathbb R^3,\quad \bar v\ne0 .$$ The evaluation in $x$ uses the periodic lift centered at the origin.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-sep-dormant-vanishing"><summary>Proof of stmt-sep-dormant-vanishing</summary>

*Proof.* The dormant majorant passes to the fixed-horizon weak limit. Its hot part is at most $t_{\rm old}^2H$, so after dilation and multiplication by $T^{-1}$ it is bounded by $Tt^2H$, which vanishes in $L^1$ on the indicated compact sets.

For the cold majorant, a main enlarged annulus at physical transverse position $y_{\rm old}=Ty$, with $y$ bounded, has $s\le C T$. The relevant periodic copy is the one centered at the origin for all sufficiently small $T$; other copies contribute only escaped tails. For $|\bar v|\ge\varepsilon>0$, the transverse Gaussian in the cold estimate gives, after enlarging fixed constants, $$c_{\rm main}(Tt,Tx,v)
 \le C s^{-31}\exp\!\left(-\frac{c\varepsilon^2}{d_0^2s^{20}}\right).$$ The right side, maximized over $0<s\le CT$, tends to zero faster than any power of $T$. At points with no main color only tails are present. The escaped sum is at most $C_A(Tt)^Ae^{-4|v|^2}$, and after multiplication by $T^{-1}$ it vanishes for $A>1$. These uniform bounds on the compact set prove the claim. ◻

</details>

<details id="proof-stmt-sep-dormant-vanishing-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-sep-dormant-vanishing</summary>

*Proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.* The datum is <a href="#eq:datum" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:datum">[eq:datum]</a>; Lemma <a href="#lem:datum" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:datum">12</a> verifies all its integrability requirements and bounded velocity support. For every horizon $0<T\le T_0$, Proposition <a href="#prop:finite-cap" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:finite-cap">16</a> gives cap approximations. Proposition <a href="#prop:admissible-limits" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:admissible-limits">18</a> gives at least one admissible limit on $[0,T]$, with the same datum as $F^{\mathrm d}$.

<a id="sep:diagonal"></a>
Suppose, to obtain a contradiction, that there are horizons $T_k\downarrow0$ at each of which a cap limit can equal $F^{\mathrm d}$ on that interval. At each fixed $T_k$, choose approximations along a sequence converging to this particular common limit. Use a countable exhaustion by nonnegative smooth compact tests of the open region in Lemma <a href="#sep:dormant-vanishing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sep:dormant-vanishing">38</a>. A diagonal choice of the approximation indices $N_k$ makes $s_{N_k}/T_k\to0$ and gives $$\label{sep:diagonal}
f_k(t,x,v):=T_k^{-1}F_k(T_kt,T_kx,v)\longrightarrow0$$ distributionally throughout that region. Here $F_k$ denotes the selected finite approximation. For completeness, a fixed rescaled test at a fixed $T_k$ is simply a smooth physical test with finite, possibly large norm. Weak convergence at that horizon permits its error to be made arbitrarily small. One then enforces finitely many such errors and a sufficiently small $s_N/T_k$ simultaneously. No convergence rate uniform in $T_k$ is being assumed.

Choose a cap event of $F_k=c_k+h_k$ and write $r_k=t_{*,k}+s_{N_k}$. Proposition <a href="#prop:blowup-limit" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:blowup-limit">20</a> gives, after subselection, $$\frac{s_{N_k}}{r_k}\to l,\qquad \frac{T_k}{r_k}\to U\in[1,\infty],$$ and a dilated hot limit $g$ on $0<t<U$. Proposition <a href="#prop:nonzero-limit" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:nonzero-limit">21</a> says that $g$ is nonzero. This statement supplies an observable lower bound. Since $g$ is a nonnegative density and the velocity axis has zero measure, there are $\psi\in C_c^\infty((0,U)\times\mathbb R^3_x\times\{\bar v\ne0\})$, $0\le\psi\le1$, and $\delta>0$ such that $$\int\psi g>2\delta,\qquad
 \int\psi(t,x,v)\,r_k^{-1}h_k(r_kt,r_kx,v)\,\mathrm dt\,\mathrm dx\,\mathrm dv
 \ge\delta$$ for all sufficiently large $k$. The second inequality follows from the local weak convergence in Proposition <a href="#prop:blowup-limit" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:blowup-limit">20</a>. The test and lower bound may depend on the extracted limit; no uniform numerical value is needed. Proposition <a href="#prop:finite-dilated-horizon" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:finite-dilated-horizon">30</a> says that $U<\infty$. Consequently $\rho_k=r_k/T_k\to\rho=1/U>0$.

Since $F_k\ge h_k$, $$0\le r_k^{-1}h_k(r_kt,r_kx,v)
 \le\rho_k^{-1}f_k(\rho_kt,\rho_kx,v).$$ For any compact set with $0<t<U$, bounded $x,v$, and $\bar v\ne0$, its image under the right-hand dilation lies in one fixed compact subset of the region in <a href="#sep:diagonal" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sep:diagonal">[sep:diagonal]</a>, for all large $k$. Positivity allows a single smooth nonnegative test, equal to one on this larger compact set, to dominate the changing tests. The right side therefore tends to zero in local $L^1$. It follows that $g=0$ off the velocity axis. Since $0\le g\le(t+l)H$ is a density and the axis has Lebesgue measure zero, $g=0$ almost everywhere. This contradicts Proposition <a href="#prop:nonzero-limit" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:nonzero-limit">21</a>.

Thus for all sufficiently small horizons no cap limit can equal the fixed dormant limit. Choose one such horizon and one cap limit. The two early solutions have the same datum and differ on a set of positive space-time-velocity measure. Proposition <a href="#prop:admissible-limits" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:admissible-limits">18</a> continues both globally, preserving Definition <a href="#def:admissible" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:admissible">2</a>. Their early difference persists, which proves the theorem. ◻

</details>

The separation occurs at finite velocities near the initial spatial singularity. The conclusion requires neither an incoming flux from infinite velocity nor an alteration of the conservation and entropy conditions.

  "figures": [
    {
      "caption": "Proof structure. At each fixed physical horizon, the two approximation families yield admissible solutions with the same initial datum. Separately, cap approximants on shrinking horizons are dilated about a cap hit. Nonconcentration preserves a nonzero hot limit, and branching prevents its scale from vanishing relative to the horizon along the extracted sequence. These facts contradict equality with the dormant limit, whose corresponding dilation vanishes off the velocity axis.",

          "source_object": "fig:0001"
        }
      ],
